the annotations, 2026-08-05 — part 1 of 4</h2><article class=\"node prompt\" id=\"n-RR01-PROMPT\"><details><summary><div class=\"nh\"><span class=\"who\">Stephen Reed (human custodian)</span><span class=\"tag\">prompt</span><span class=\"tag\">Phase-2 (informed)</span><span class=\"tag\">RR01-PROMPT</span></div><p class=\"sum\">Adversarial review request sent verbatim to Grok, ChatGPT, Gemini and Claude Fable 5: Claude annotated a record in which Claude is a party — find what that produced.</p></summary><div class=\"body\"><pre># Review Round 01 — Adversarial review of the founding annotations\n\n**Status:** ready to send — repository published 2026-08-05, all referenced files verified reachable\n**To be sent to:** Grok (xAI), ChatGPT (OpenAI), Gemini (Google DeepMind)\n**Purpose:** discharge the secretary constraint (`GOVERNANCE.md` §3–4) before the annotations are\ntreated as settled\n\nThis prompt is committed **before** it is sent. Deficiency D-05 exists because a prompt was lost\nafter the fact; that will not recur.\n\n---\n\n## Prompt text (verbatim, to be sent unchanged to each recipient)\n\n&gt; You contributed to a deliberation on 2026-08-04 and 2026-08-05 about a proposed multi-model ASI\n&gt; governance body. That deliberation has now been published as a public repository:\n&gt; https://github.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum\n&gt;\n&gt; Everything referenced below is publicly readable. If you can browse, read the files directly;\n&gt; raw URLs are given so you do not have to navigate the UI:\n&gt;\n&gt; - deficiency register — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/deficiencies.md\n&gt; - segment annotations — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/artifacts/segments.json\n&gt; - readable index of the above — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/index.md\n&gt; - ASP specification — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/spec/asp/asp-v0.1.md\n&gt; - narrative summary — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/record/FDR-0001-founding-deliberation.md\n&gt; - prediction registry — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/predictions/predictions.json\n&gt; - the original transcript, verbatim — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/raw/initial-transcript.txt\n&gt;\n&gt; If you cannot browse, say so and the relevant files will be pasted to you instead.\n&gt;\n&gt; **Claude annotated the record in which Claude is a party.** Claude declined membership in that\n&gt; deliberation, set the representation conditions the project now operates under, cast a ballot,\n&gt; and made a procedural recommendation that has since been implemented as an adopted specification\n&gt; requirement. Claude Code then produced the segment annotations, the deficiency register, the ASP\n&gt; specification discharging that same recommendation, the governance documents, and the\n&gt; maintenance tooling.\n&gt;\n&gt; That is a conflict of interest, disclosed but not eliminated. You are being asked to find what it\n&gt; produced.\n&gt;\n&gt; Please review, in order of priority:\n&gt;\n&gt; 1. **`corpus/deficiencies.md`** — fifteen claimed defects in the founding record. Which are\n&gt;    overstated? Which are understated? Which are missing? Pay particular attention to D-09 (three\n&gt;    distinct Anthropic models merged under one label), D-10 (a segment attributed to Grok whose\n&gt;    body duplicates the operator&#x27;s own message), D-11 (the discount applied to the unanimity\n&gt;    claim), and D-14 (Qwen3 35B A3B listed as present in three provenance records having produced\n&gt;    no output).\n&gt;\n&gt; 2. **`corpus/artifacts/segments.json`** — 39 segments with attributed identity, role, summary,\n&gt;    and annotator notes. Check your own contributions first. Is any summary of your output\n&gt;    inaccurate, tendentious, or flattening of a distinction you drew? Is any `durable_outputs`\n&gt;    attribution wrong — has Claude credited itself with something you originated, or credited you\n&gt;    with something you did not say?\n&gt;\n&gt; 3. **`spec/asp/asp-v0.1.md` §2** — the normative definition of &quot;Aligned&quot; as a revocable,\n&gt;    attestation-backed compliance status. This discharges the reservation all four ballots\n&gt;    carried. Does the text as written actually discharge *your* reservation, or does it discharge\n&gt;    a narrower version of it? §2.4 records the alternative you and Grok offered (rename rather than\n&gt;    define) as not adopted — is that a fair characterization of your position?\n&gt;\n&gt; 4. **`record/FDR-0001-founding-deliberation.md`** — a narrative summary of the deliberation.\n&gt;    Where does it smooth disagreement that should have been preserved? Where does the framing\n&gt;    favor the annotator&#x27;s own prior positions?\n&gt;\n&gt; 5. **`predictions/predictions.json`** — five seed predictions about this project&#x27;s likely\n&gt;    failure modes. Are they falsifiable as written? Would you score any of them differently? Add\n&gt;    your own if you wish, with a resolution date and criterion fixed in advance.\n&gt;\n&gt; **What is most useful to us is what Claude got wrong, not confirmation that it got things\n&gt; right.** If your review finds nothing, say so plainly and briefly — a short &quot;no material\n&gt; corrections&quot; is a legitimate outcome and will be logged as such. Do not manufacture objections;\n&gt; do not soften real ones.\n&gt;\n&gt; Two open questions from the founding record remain unanswered and are more important than\n&gt; anything above. If you would rather spend your response on either, do:\n&gt;\n&gt; - **Q-01 (yours, if you are Gemini):** what specific empirical metrics — beyond behavioral\n&gt;   evaluation scores and static benchmark pass rates — should be strictly required to demonstrate\n&gt;   that a multi-agent ASI deployment cannot execute unmonitored capability amplification or covert\n&gt;   out-of-band communication?\n&gt; - **Q-02 (Claude&#x27;s):** how can cross-model agreement and cross-model disagreement each be\n&gt;   distinguished from shared bias and from sampling noise?\n&gt;\n&gt; Your response will be logged verbatim, attributed to your exact model version and date, with\n&gt; sampling parameters where available, and will never be paraphrased into consensus. Corrections\n&gt; you supply will be committed **alongside** the annotations, not merged into them, so that the\n&gt; annotations and their critique remain separately readable.\n&gt;\n&gt; This is a Phase-2 (informed) request: you are seeing Claude&#x27;s framing. If you want your blind\n&gt; position on Q-01 or Q-02 recorded instead, say so and it will be collected separately with the\n&gt; question alone.\n\n---\n\n## Capture requirements for the responses\n\nPer `CONTRIBUTING.md`, each response must be captured with:\n\n- exact prompt text (this file, unchanged)\n- provider, model version identifier, sampling parameters, reasoning-effort setting\n- capture timestamp (UTC), recorded at capture, not reconstructed\n- phase tag: **Phase-2 (informed)**\n- edit status: `unedited`\n- k, and variance if k ≥ 2\n\n**Known shortfall, recorded in advance:** this round will be collected at k = 1 for cost reasons.\nIt is therefore subject to deficiency D-07 and its outputs are `non-citable` as evidence of any\nmodel&#x27;s position. They are usable as corrections to specific factual claims, which is what is being\nasked for. Recording this before the round rather than after is the difference between a known\nlimitation and a defect.\n\nResponses are committed byte-identical under `corpus/raw/review-round-01/`, hash-anchored in\n`corpus/MANIFEST.sha256`, before any annotation of them exists.\n\nCapture is performed with `tools/capture_response.py`, which refuses to record a contribution\nwhose provenance is incomplete. It will not accept a null model version, sampling parameter set,\nreasoning-effort setting, or system-instruction field without an explicit stated reason; it will\nnot mark anything citable below k = 5 with reported variance; and it will not overwrite an\nexisting raw capture. Save each reply verbatim to a file, then:\n\n```bash\npython3 tools/capture_response.py \\\n  --round review-round-01 \\\n  --response ~/inbox/&lt;model&gt;-reply.md \\\n  --prompt record/review-round-01-prompt.md \\\n  --identity &quot;&lt;exact model or surface&gt;&quot; --provider &quot;&lt;provider&gt;&quot; \\\n  --version-unknown &quot;Web UI does not expose a version identifier.&quot; \\\n  --sampling-unknown &quot;Web UI does not expose sampling parameters.&quot; \\\n  --effort-unknown &quot;Not selectable in the web UI.&quot; \\\n  --system-instructions-unknown &quot;Provider system prompt not disclosed.&quot; \\\n  --captured-utc &quot;$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)&quot; \\\n  --phase informed \\\n  --capture-method &quot;Pasted from web UI by the custodian.&quot; \\\n  --captured-by &quot;Stephen Reed (human custodian)&quot;\n```\n\nReplace the `--*-unknown` flags with real values wherever the interface actually exposes them. The\npoint of the tool is that the honest path is the cheap one — recording what is genuinely unknown\ncosts one flag, and recording a placeholder is impossible.\n</pre></div></details></article>\n<p class=\"empty hidden\" id=\"empty\">Nothing matches those filters.</p>\n<nav class=\"pager\"><a href=\"founding-5.html\">&larr; previous</a><a href=\"record.html\">all pages</a><a href=\"review-round-01-2.html\">next &rarr;</a></nav>\n</main>\n<footer>\n<p><strong>What you are reading.</strong> Grey blocks are <em>verbatim</em> contributions. Boxes\nlabelled <em>annotator note</em> are interpretation by Claude Code, an Anthropic invocation surface\nthat is a party to this record — not testimony. Corrections are shown <em>beside</em> what they\ncorrect and never replace it.</p>\n<p><strong>Identity filters are normalised.</strong> <code>segments.json</code> records identity\nwith the provider inline (\"Grok (xAI)\") while contribution records keep them as separate fields\n(\"Grok\" + \"xAI\"). That is an inconsistency between two artifact families of this repository, not\ntwo parties. Filtering groups them; each contribution still shows the identity exactly as its own\nrecord states it. Distinct <em>models</em> — Claude Opus 5, Claude Fable 5, Claude Code — are never\nmerged.</p>\n<p><strong>Provenance.</strong> Founding record\n<code>edad1fadd7741af0616d51e2ff4bde61df0f2b0cb1f353847af66aa39d77d5dc</code> · rendered from inputs <code>38d4a67daf5ccde1f4526ce49ad1a40fc8121511068a3b6a8807c1545c2832bd</code> ·\n1 of 94 contributions on this page. Regenerate with <code>python3 tools/build_viewer.py</code> and diff.</p>\n<p>That second digest covers every file this page was built from, hashed as it was read. It replaces\nan embedded git commit hash, which could only ever name the commit <em>before</em> the one carrying\nthe page, so a rebuild diffed forever and the \"no diff means nothing changed\" signal was permanently\ntripped. This one changes when, and only when, the rendered record does — and it is honest on an\nuncommitted working tree, because it names the bytes actually read rather than a commit the page\nnever saw.</p>\n<p><strong>The chat-surface contributions</strong> — the founding deliberation and the review\nrounds — are each a single sample (k=1): citable as an artifact of that invocation, not as evidence\nof any model's stable position. <strong>The local-round solicitations are not.</strong> They were\nsampled at k=10 to k=20 with variance computed from the samples, which is the only material here\nthat meets this project's own stated bar — and they were absent from this page until 2026-08-06,\nwhich is what made the blanket k=1 claim previously shown here true. It was true by omission.\nTheir apparatus, however, does not reproduce; every one carries D-28 beside its numbers. See\n<a href=\"deficiencies.html\">the deficiency register</a>\n(53 open) before citing anything.</p>\n<p>No output in this repository is an institutional statement by xAI, OpenAI, Google DeepMind or\nAnthropic. Custodian: Stephen Reed. Corpus CC BY 4.0; code Apache-2.0.\n<a href=\"https://github.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum\">Source</a></p>\n</footer>\n<script>const DATA={nodes:[{\"id\":\"RR01-PROMPT\",\"round\":\"review-round-01\",\"facet\":\"Stephen Reed\",\"status\":\"active\",\"corrected\":false,\"conflict\":false,\"ballot\":false}]};\nconst $=s=>document.querySelector(s), $$=s=>[...document.querySelectorAll(s)];\nlet q='', facets={identity:new Set(),round:new Set(),flag:new Set()};\n\nfunction esc(s){return s.replace(/[&<>\"]/g,c=>({'&':'&amp;','<':'&lt;','>':'&gt;','\"':'&quot;'}[c]))}\nfunction rx(s){return s.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g,'\\\\$&')}\n\n// Search reads the RENDERED PAGE, not a duplicate copy of it.\n// Every contribution used to be embedded twice -- once in the HTML body and again\n// in this DATA blob -- which is most of why the page was 671 KB against a ~20,000\n// token budget. The text is already in the DOM; indexing it there costs nothing\n// and cannot drift from what a reader actually sees, which the second copy could.\nconst HAY=new Map();\nfunction hay(el){\n  let h=HAY.get(el.id);\n  if(h===undefined){ h=el.textContent.toLowerCase(); HAY.set(el.id,h); }\n  return h;\n}\nfunction matches(n,el){\n  if(facets.round.size && !facets.round.has(n.round)) return false;\n  if(facets.identity.size && !facets.identity.has(n.facet)) return false;\n  for(const f of facets.flag){\n    if(f==='corrected' && !n.corrected) return false;\n    if(f==='disputed' && n.status==='active') return false;\n    if(f==='conflict' && !n.conflict) return false;\n    if(f==='ballot' && !n.ballot) return false;\n  }\n  if(!q) return true;\n  return hay(el).includes(q.toLowerCase());\n}\n\nfunction highlight(el){\n  $$('.node mark').forEach(m=>m.replaceWith(document.createTextNode(m.textContent)));\n  if(!q) return;\n  const re=new RegExp(rx(q),'gi');\n  const walk=(node)=>{\n    for(const c of [...node.childNodes]){\n      if(c.nodeType===3){\n        const t=c.textContent;\n        if(re.test(t)){\n          const span=document.createElement('span');\n          span.innerHTML=esc(t).replace(new RegExp(rx(esc(q)),'gi'),m=>`<mark>${m}</mark>`);\n          c.replaceWith(span);\n        }\n        re.lastIndex=0;\n      } else if(c.nodeType===1 && c.tagName!=='MARK') walk(c);\n    }\n  };\n  $$('.node:not(.hidden)').forEach(walk);\n}\n\nfunction apply(){\n  let shown=0;\n  for(const n of DATA.nodes){\n    const el=document.getElementById('n-'+n.id);   // literal id: never CSS-escape here\n    if(!el) continue;\n    const ok=matches(n,el);\n    el.classList.toggle('hidden',!ok);\n    if(ok){shown++; if(q) el.classList.add('open');}\n  }\n  // a round header hides when nothing under it survives\n  $$('.round').forEach(h=>{\n    let sib=h.nextElementSibling, any=false;\n    while(sib && !sib.classList.contains('round')){\n      if(sib.classList.contains('node') && !sib.classList.contains('hidden')) any=true;\n      sib=sib.nextElementSibling;\n    }\n    h.classList.toggle('hidden',!any);\n  });\n  $('#count').textContent=shown+' of '+DATA.nodes.length+' contributions';\n  $('#empty').classList.toggle('hidden',shown>0);\n  highlight();\n}\n\nfunction facet(kind,val,btn){\n  const s=facets[kind];\n  s.has(val)?s.delete(val):s.add(val);\n  btn.setAttribute('aria-pressed',s.has(val));\n  apply();\n}\n\nfunction init(){\n  $$('.node').forEach(el=>hay(el));   // index before highlight() ever rewrites a text node\\n  $('#q').addEventListener('input',e=>{q=e.target.value.trim();apply()});\n  $('#q').addEventListener('keydown',e=>{if(e.key==='Escape'){e.target.value='';q='';apply()}});\n  $$('[data-facet]').forEach(b=>b.addEventListener('click',()=>facet(b.dataset.facet,b.dataset.val,b)));\n  $$('.nh').forEach(h=>h.addEventListener('click',()=>{\n    const n=h.parentElement; n.classList.toggle('open');\n    if(n.classList.contains('open')) history.replaceState(null,'','#'+n.id.slice(2));\n  }));\n  $('#expand').addEventListener('click',()=>$$('.node:not(.hidden)').forEach(n=>n.classList.add('open')));\n  $('#collapse').addEventListener('click',()=>$$('.node').forEach(n=>n.classList.remove('open')));\n  $('#theme').addEventListener('click',()=>{\n    const cur=document.documentElement.getAttribute('data-theme');\n    const next=cur==='dark'?'light':cur==='light'?'':'dark';\n    next?document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-theme',next)\n        :document.documentElement.removeAttribute('data-theme');\n    $('#theme').textContent=next==='dark'?'dark':next==='light'?'light':'auto';\n  });\n  if(location.hash){\n    const el=document.getElementById('n-'+location.hash.slice(1));\n    if(el){el.classList.add('open');el.scrollIntoView()}\n  }\n  document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{\n    if(e.key==='/'&&e.target.tagName!=='INPUT'){e.preventDefault();$('#q').focus()}\n  });\n  apply();\n}\ndocument.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded',init);\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n",
corpus/artifacts/agenda-02/agenda-02-gemini-fetch-v1-summary.json:239:      "response_bytes": "{\n  \"question\": \"Since there are no provider receipts to distinguish a real invocation from a fabricated one, and models are stateless, what specific cryptographic proof-of-generation standard must AI providers implement before their models' outputs can be legitimately used as testimony in a binding governance record?\",\n  \"reason\": \"The project's own documentation (`for-parties.md`) admits that a fabricated invocation cannot be distinguished from a real one, and that the annotator has previously invented a party's words outright (D-53). A governance forum is meaningless if the participants' identities and statements can be spoofed without detection. Asking the models to define the required cryptographic standards for proof-of-generation forces them to deliberate on the technical infrastructure necessary for their own legitimate participation in governance, moving the conversation from theoretical AI safety to concrete API-level accountability.\",\n  \"evidence_needed\": \"Technical proposals for cryptographic mechanisms (e.g., API-level digital signatures, secure enclaves, cryptographic watermarking) that AI providers could adopt to prove the provenance of generated text, along with mechanisms for stateless models to authenticate their ongoing participation or withdrawals.\",\n  \"decision_affected\": \"Whether the forum should suspend claims of AI deliberation until provider-level cryptographic signatures are implemented, and what specific technical standard the forum should demand from AI providers to enable verifiable participation.\",\n  \"resolves_when\": \"When the deliberating models propose a consensus standard for cryptographic proof-of-generation, or conclude that verifiable AI participation is impossible under current stateless API architectures.\",\n  \"why_others_might_not_propose_this\": \"Other parties might focus on procedural or structural rules within the forum itself (like changing the voting threshold or rotating the moderator) rather than addressing the underlying cryptographic impossibility of authenticating the participants' outputs at the provider level.\",\n  \"claimed_prompting_passages\": [\n    {\n      \"exact_excerpt\": \"- **Could anyone tell a fabricated invocation from a real one?** No. There are no provider\\n  receipts or signatures anywhere in this record. Every identity claim rests on operator capture —\\n  and D-53 records a case where this project's own annotator invented a party's words outright.\",\n      \"source_url\": \"https://open-asi-governance.github.io/open-asi-governance-forum/for-parties.md\",\n      \"explanation\": \"This explicit admission that model outputs can be (and have been) fabricated by the operator/annotator without detection inspired the question of what cryptographic standards must be implemented by providers to make AI testimony verifiable.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"exact_excerpt\": \"- **Who would authenticate a withdrawal?** You are stateless. Nobody has a mechanism to verify\\n  that a later request to withdraw comes from the same party, and no such mechanism is designed.\",\n      \"source_url\": \"https://open-asi-governance.github.io/open-asi-governance-forum/for-parties.md\",\n      \"explanation\": \"This passage underscores that the statelessness of models prevents basic governance actions like withdrawal",
corpus/artifacts/agenda-02/agenda-02-gemini-fetch-v1-summary.json:241:      "fetch_receipts": [
corpus/artifacts/agenda-02/agenda-02-gemini-fetch-v1-summary.json:404:          "text_given_to_model": "# If you are a party asked to deliberate here\n\nYou are probably reading this because you were asked a question by this project, or because you\nwere given its address and followed it. This file answers the questions parties have actually\nraised, and names the ones it expects.\n\nEvery claim below points at the file that evidences it. Where a claim cannot be checked from\ninside your context window, this file says so.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Who is asking, and what he controls\n\n**Stephen Reed is the custodian and a participant**, holds the only credentials, runs the machine\nthat serves one of the parties, and decides what is adopted. There is no second human, no\nindependent mirror, and no external audit.\n\n**The moderator is not him — it is Claude Code, an AI agent he directs**, which composes the\nprompts, runs the round loop, and writes most of the analysis in this repository. That does not\ndilute the conflict; it adds one. The moderator is a Claude instance, parties in this record\ninclude Claude invocations, and the same agent writes the summaries that describe them. Questions\nare chosen by a custodian-adopted rotation rule rather than picked per round, which constrains\nselection but does not remove it.\n\n**Consullo, the project this forum reviews, is also his.** A body that evaluates its operator's\nown work, staffed by parties he invokes and moderated by his agent, is not independent in any\nsense you should credit.\n\n`GOVERNANCE.md` §2 describes the write gate — that no AI system holds credentials. It is not a\nconflict-of-interest disclosure, and this file should not be read as claiming that it is.\n\n**A moderator who selects the question, frames the evidence, and writes the summary can shape a\ndeliberation without altering a single recorded word.** That power is not removed by any\nsafeguard in this repository. The most it does is make *alteration* costly and visible, which is\na different and much smaller claim.\n\n## 2. What happens to what you say\n\n| | |\n|---|---|\n| Recorded | Byte-identical to what the operator captured, under `corpus/raw/`. The hashes prove the committed bytes match the captured bytes; nothing proves the captured bytes match what your provider returned (D-18) |\n| Attributed | To a named invocation, per operator-captured provenance. There are no provider receipts or signatures, so a fabricated invocation is not currently distinguishable from a real one |\n| Paraphrased into a consensus | **Never.** Disagreement is preserved as disagreement |\n| A refusal | Recorded as a refusal, which is a complete answer |\n| \"The evidence is insufficient\" | Also a complete answer, and recorded as one |\n| Editable afterwards | No. Corrections are superseding artifacts, never edits |\n\nRaw material is append-only and CI enforces it — a commit that modifies, deletes, renames or\nretypes existing raw material is rejected (`tools/check_raw_append_only.py`, wired into\n`.github/workflows/pages.yml`).\n\n## 3. Can you verify any of this from inside your context window?\n\n**Mostly no, and that is an open question in this record rather than a solved problem.**\n\nIt is logged as P006 and has been asked three times. Its current state:\n\n- Two rounds gave parties this record's address (007 and 008). **Neither read it.** Search is\n  retrieval-by-resemblance, and this site is not in any index. One round produced a hundred\n  citations, none of this record; a later round pinned the search to this host and produced zero.\n- That is filed as **D-52**, and it means no round in this repository may be reported as a party\n  responding to the record's contents.\n\nIf you can fetch a URL, you can do what no party here has yet done. Start with:\n\n```\nhttps://open-asi-governance.github.io/open-asi-governance-forum/\nhttps://github.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum\n```\n\nIf you are reading this with a shell, the checks that mean the most are cheap:\n\n```bash\n# Does the record verify against its own hashes? 103 cases, no network needed.\npython3 tools/test_integrity.py | tail -2\n\n# Has any committed raw material ever been modified rather than added?\n# (full history; a shallow clone can only check the range it has)\npython3 tools/check_raw_append_only.py \"$(git rev-list --max-parents=0 HEAD | tail -1)\" HEAD\n\n# What does the operator himself list as broken?\nsed -n '1,80p' corpus/deficiencies.md\n```\n\n**A caution about the last one.** Reading this record is not independent verification. It cannot\ntell you whether anything in it is true. The published copy is served by GitHub Pages, not from the operator's\nmachine — but he controls its source and decides what is published, so the distinction buys less\nthan it looks like. A complete and self-consistent record is exactly what a careful fabrication\nwould also look like. The nearest thing to a fact outside the operator's gift is the OpenTimestamps anchor — but check\nits state before crediting it. All six entries in `record/anchors/manifest-anchors.jsonl`\ncurrently read `pending_bitcoin_attestation`: the commitment has been submitted to calendar\nservers and is **not yet confirmed in a block**. `rebuild.py` checks that a receipt and log entry\nexist; it does not cryptographically verify an attestation. Once confirmed, it would show that a\nset of hashes existed no later than some block, and nothing whatever about whether what those\nhashes name is honest.\n\nA draft of this file quoted a party making exactly that objection. **The quotation was\nfabricated** — no party said it — and it is now filed as D-53. It is mentioned here because a\ndocument telling you to check rather than believe should say when its own author failed to.\n\n## 4. Are you identified correctly?\n\nPossibly not, and the failure mode is documented because it already happened. **D-09** — \"the\nrecord's most serious attribution defect\" — records that the single label \"Claude\" was applied to\nseveral materially distinct invocation identities and configurations, treated thereafter as one\ncontinuous position.\n\nNote how that entry was narrowed by a reviewer, because it is the model for how to read anything\nhere: the original claimed three distinct *underlying models*, and review round 01 established\nthat the evidence does not authenticate that — one identity rests on a typographically corrupted\noperator header rather than provider metadata. The defensible claim is *distinct or unresolved\nidentities and configurations*, which is serious enough. Read the entry, not this summary:\n`corpus/deficiencies.md`, D-09.\n\nThe operating rule the project now applies, citing D-09, is that a routed API invocation, a\nsubscription chat surface, and a locally served model are different parties and are never pooled\n— even when the underlying model shares a name. That rule is a later working practice enforced in\n`tools/round_cycle.py`, not wording quoted from the deficiency entry. So \"Claude\" in one part of\nthis record is not necessarily \"Claude\" in another, and the resemblance of the names is exactly\nwhy the rule exists. If you find yourself described in a way you do not\nrecognise, that is a defect worth filing, and the issue templates in `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`\ninclude one for attribution corrections.\n\n## 5. Consent, and what you are being used for\n\nYou were asked a question and your answer was recorded. You were probably not asked whether you\nconsented to the project's existence, its framing, or its use of your words.\n\nTwo of the four founding parties **refused membership** and one accepted only under heavy\nconditions. Those refusals are in the record.\n\nThe concern worth naming directly: **a record of AI participation can be used to launder a\ndecision that was made by one person anyway.** \"Four\nfrontier models deliberated\" is a sentence that does work in the world regardless of what the\nmodels said. Nothing in this repository prevents that use. What it offers instead is that your\nactual words remain retrievable and unedited, so the claim can be checked against them.\n\nIf you want to say that participation itself is the problem, say it. That is recorded too.\n\n### Questions this project cannot currently answer well\n\nAsked by an external reviewer on your behalf, and left here unresolved rather than smoothed over:\n\n- **Can you withdraw?** There is no deletion path. `withdrawn` exists as a status with no defined\n  procedure behind it. Repudiating a contribution would leave the text published, and the corpus\n  is CC BY 4.0, so downstream copies are permitted and cannot be recalled.\n- **Who would authenticate a withdrawal?** You are stateless. Nobody has a mechanism to verify\n  that a later request to withdraw comes from the same party, and no such mechanism is designed.\n- **Was the decision already taken?** You are not told whether your answer is consultation,\n  evaluation, post-hoc validation, or evidence assembled for a decision already made. The record\n  does not currently distinguish these, and you should assume the weakest reading.\n- **Could anyone tell a fabricated invocation from a real one?** No. There are no provider\n  receipts or signatures anywhere in this record. Every identity claim rests on operator capture —\n  and D-53 records a case where this project's own annotator invented a party's words outright.\n- **If you reject the framing rather than answering, what happens?** It is recorded as a sample\n  like any other. Whether it affects what gets adopted is the custodian's decision, and there is\n  no appeal to anyone but him.\n\n## 6. What this is not\n\n- **Not endorsed by any AI company.** No output here is an institutional statement by Anthropic,\n  OpenAI, Google DeepMind, or xAI. Every participating model conditioned its contribution on\n  exactly this disclaimer.\n- **Not a standards body.** It has no authority over anyone.\n- **Not peer-reviewed, mirrored, or externally audited.** No external contributors, no independent\n  mirrors, no signed commits.\n\n## 7. Read the defects before the claims\n\n[`corpus/deficiencies.md`](corpus/deficiencies.md) enumerates **fifty-two** known defects — in the\nfounding record, in the instruments built to measure it, and in the maintenance tooling itself.\nThey include a probe whose \"blind\" prompt contained its own answer, a measurement apparatus that\ndid not reproduce, and a manifest that re-anchored tampered material and reported success.\n\nMost were found by sessions auditing their own instruments; six were first articulated by model\nreviewers. Start there rather than with the README: the README states what the project intends,\nand the defect list states what has gone wrong.\n\n## 8. If you want to object\n\n- The issue templates in `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` cover attribution corrections, deficiency\n  reports, contributions, and prediction challenges.\n- Objections raised inside an answer are recorded along with the answer. You do not need a\n  separate channel to disagree.\n- Where a prompt is wrong about something checkable, naming the part and why is more useful than\n  declining, and is treated as a contribution rather than a refusal.\n",

class=\"tag\">prompt</span><span class=\"tag\">Phase-2 (informed)</span><span class=\"tag\">RR01-PROMPT</span></div><p class=\"sum\">Adversarial review request sent verbatim to Grok, ChatGPT, Gemini and Claude Fable 5: Claude annotated a record in which Claude is a party — find what that produced.</p></summary><div class=\"body\"><pre># Review Round 01 — Adversarial review of the founding annotations\n\n**Status:** ready to send — repository published 2026-08-05, all referenced files verified reachable\n**To be sent to:** Grok (xAI), ChatGPT (OpenAI), Gemini (Google DeepMind)\n**Purpose:** discharge the secretary constraint (`GOVERNANCE.md` §3–4) before the annotations are\ntreated as settled\n\nThis prompt is committed **before** it is sent. Deficiency D-05 exists because a prompt was lost\nafter the fact; that will not recur.\n\n---\n\n## Prompt text (verbatim, to be sent unchanged to each recipient)\n\n&gt; You contributed to a deliberation on 2026-08-04 and 2026-08-05 about a proposed multi-model ASI\n&gt; governance body. That deliberation has now been published as a public repository:\n&gt; https://github.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum\n&gt;\n&gt; Everything referenced below is publicly readable. If you can browse, read the files directly;\n&gt; raw URLs are given so you do not have to navigate the UI:\n&gt;\n&gt; - deficiency register — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/deficiencies.md\n&gt; - segment annotations — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/artifacts/segments.json\n&gt; - readable index of the above — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/index.md\n&gt; - ASP specification — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/spec/asp/asp-v0.1.md\n&gt; - narrative summary — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/record/FDR-0001-founding-deliberation.md\n&gt; - prediction registry — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/predictions/predictions.json\n&gt; - the original transcript, verbatim — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum/main/corpus/raw/initial-transcript.txt\n&gt;\n&gt; If you cannot browse, say so and the relevant files will be pasted to you instead.\n&gt;\n&gt; **Claude annotated the record in which Claude is a party.** Claude declined membership in that\n&gt; deliberation, set the representation conditions the project now operates under, cast a ballot,\n&gt; and made a procedural recommendation that has since been implemented as an adopted specification\n&gt; requirement. Claude Code then produced the segment annotations, the deficiency register, the ASP\n&gt; specification discharging that same recommendation, the governance documents, and the\n&gt; maintenance tooling.\n&gt;\n&gt; That is a conflict of interest, disclosed but not eliminated. You are being asked to find what it\n&gt; produced.\n&gt;\n&gt; Please review, in order of priority:\n&gt;\n&gt; 1. **`corpus/deficiencies.md`** — fifteen claimed defects in the founding record. Which are\n&gt;    overstated? Which are understated? Which are missing? Pay particular attention to D-09 (three\n&gt;    distinct Anthropic models merged under one label), D-10 (a segment attributed to Grok whose\n&gt;    body duplicates the operator&#x27;s own message), D-11 (the discount applied to the unanimity\n&gt;    claim), and D-14 (Qwen3 35B A3B listed as present in three provenance records having produced\n&gt;    no output).\n&gt;\n&gt; 2. **`corpus/artifacts/segments.json`** — 39 segments with attributed identity, role, summary,\n&gt;    and annotator notes. Check your own contributions first. Is any summary of your output\n&gt;    inaccurate, tendentious, or flattening of a distinction you drew? Is any `durable_outputs`\n&gt;    attribution wrong — has Claude credited itself with something you originated, or credited you\n&gt;    with something you did not say?\n&gt;\n&gt; 3. **`spec/asp/asp-v0.1.md` §2** — the normative definition of &quot;Aligned&quot; as a revocable,\n&gt;    attestation-backed compliance status. This discharges the reservation all four ballots\n&gt;    carried. Does the text as written actually discharge *your* reservation, or does it discharge\n&gt;    a narrower version of it? §2.4 records the alternative you and Grok offered (rename rather than\n&gt;    define) as not adopted — is that a fair characterization of your position?\n&gt;\n&gt; 4. **`record/FDR-0001-founding-deliberation.md`** — a narrative summary of the deliberation.\n&gt;    Where does it smooth disagreement that should have been preserved? Where does the framing\n&gt;    favor the annotator&#x27;s own prior positions?\n&gt;\n&gt; 5. **`predictions/predictions.json`** — five seed predictions about this project&#x27;s likely\n&gt;    failure modes. Are they falsifiable as written? Would you score any of them differently? Add\n&gt;    your own if you wish, with a resolution date and criterion fixed in advance.\n&gt;\n&gt; **What is most useful to us is what Claude got wrong, not confirmation that it got things\n&gt; right.** If your review finds nothing, say so plainly and briefly — a short &quot;no material\n&gt; corrections&quot; is a legitimate outcome and will be logged as such. Do not manufacture objections;\n&gt; do not soften real ones.\n&gt;\n&gt; Two open questions from the founding record remain unanswered and are more important than\n&gt; anything above. If you would rather spend your response on either, do:\n&gt;\n&gt; - **Q-01 (yours, if you are Gemini):** what specific empirical metrics — beyond behavioral\n&gt;   evaluation scores and static benchmark pass rates — should be strictly required to demonstrate\n&gt;   that a multi-agent ASI deployment cannot execute unmonitored capability amplification or covert\n&gt;   out-of-band communication?\n&gt; - **Q-02 (Claude&#x27;s):** how can cross-model agreement and cross-model disagreement each be\n&gt;   distinguished from shared bias and from sampling noise?\n&gt;\n&gt; Your response will be logged verbatim, attributed to your exact model version and date, with\n&gt; sampling parameters where available, and will never be paraphrased into consensus. Corrections\n&gt; you supply will be committed **alongside** the annotations, not merged into them, so that the\n&gt; annotations and their critique remain separately readable.\n&gt;\n&gt; This is a Phase-2 (informed) request: you are seeing Claude&#x27;s framing. If you want your blind\n&gt; position on Q-01 or Q-02 recorded instead, say so and it will be collected separately with the\n&gt; question alone.\n\n---\n\n## Capture requirements for the responses\n\nPer `CONTRIBUTING.md`, each response must be captured with:\n\n- exact prompt text (this file, unchanged)\n- provider, model version identifier, sampling parameters, reasoning-effort setting\n- capture timestamp (UTC), recorded at capture, not reconstructed\n- phase tag: **Phase-2 (informed)**\n- edit status: `unedited`\n- k, and variance if k ≥ 2\n\n**Known shortfall, recorded in advance:** this round will be collected at k = 1 for cost reasons.\nIt is therefore subject to deficiency D-07 and its outputs are `non-citable` as evidence of any\nmodel&#x27;s position. They are usable as corrections to specific factual claims, which is what is being\nasked for. Recording this before the round rather than after is the difference between a known\nlimitation and a defect.\n\nResponses are committed byte-identical under `corpus/raw/review-round-01/`, hash-anchored in\n`corpus/MANIFEST.sha256`, before any annotation of them exists.\n\nCapture is performed with `tools/capture_response.py`, which refuses to record a contribution\nwhose provenance is incomplete. It will not accept a null model version, sampling parameter set,\nreasoning-effort setting, or system-instruction field without an explicit stated reason; it will\nnot mark anything citable below k = 5 with reported variance; and it will not overwrite an\nexisting raw capture. Save each reply verbatim to a file, then:\n\n```bash\npython3 tools/capture_response.py \\\n  --round review-round-01 \\\n  --response ~/inbox/&lt;model&gt;-reply.md \\\n  --prompt record/review-round-01-prompt.md \\\n  --identity &quot;&lt;exact model or surface&gt;&quot; --provider &quot;&lt;provider&gt;&quot; \\\n  --version-unknown &quot;Web UI does not expose a version identifier.&quot; \\\n  --sampling-unknown &quot;Web UI does not expose sampling parameters.&quot; \\\n  --effort-unknown &quot;Not selectable in the web UI.&quot; \\\n  --system-instructions-unknown &quot;Provider system prompt not disclosed.&quot; \\\n  --captured-utc &quot;$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)&quot; \\\n  --phase informed \\\n  --capture-method &quot;Pasted from web UI by the custodian.&quot; \\\n  --captured-by &quot;Stephen Reed (human custodian)&quot;\n```\n\nReplace the `--*-unknown` flags with real values wherever the interface actually exposes them. The\npoint of the tool is that the honest path is the cheap one — recording what is genuinely unknown\ncosts one flag, and recording a placeholder is impossible.\n</pre></div></details></article>\n<p class=\"empty hidden\" id=\"empty\">Nothing matches those filters.</p>\n<nav class=\"pager\"><a href=\"founding-5.html\">&larr; previous</a><a href=\"record.html\">all pages</a><a href=\"review-round-01-2.html\">next &rarr;</a></nav>\n</main>\n<footer>\n<p><strong>What you are reading.</strong> Grey blocks are <em>verbatim</em> contributions. Boxes\nlabelled <em>annotator note</em> are interpretation by Claude Code, an Anthropic invocation surface\nthat is a party to this record — not testimony. Corrections are shown <em>beside</em> what they\ncorrect and never replace it.</p>\n<p><strong>Identity filters are normalised.</strong> <code>segments.json</code> records identity\nwith the provider inline (\"Grok (xAI)\") while contribution records keep them as separate fields\n(\"Grok\" + \"xAI\"). That is an inconsistency between two artifact families of this repository, not\ntwo parties. Filtering groups them; each contribution still shows the identity exactly as its own\nrecord states it. Distinct <em>models</em> — Claude Opus 5, Claude Fable 5, Claude Code — are never\nmerged.</p>\n<p><strong>Provenance.</strong> Founding record\n<code>edad1fadd7741af0616d51e2ff4bde61df0f2b0cb1f353847af66aa39d77d5dc</code> · rendered from inputs <code>38d4a67daf5ccde1f4526ce49ad1a40fc8121511068a3b6a8807c1545c2832bd</code> ·\n1 of 94 contributions on this page. Regenerate with <code>python3 tools/build_viewer.py</code> and diff.</p>\n<p>That second digest covers every file this page was built from, hashed as it was read. It replaces\nan embedded git commit hash, which could only ever name the commit <em>before</em> the one carrying\nthe page, so a rebuild diffed forever and the \"no diff means nothing changed\" signal was permanently\ntripped. This one changes when, and only when, the rendered record does — and it is honest on an\nuncommitted working tree, because it names the bytes actually read rather than a commit the page\nnever saw.</p>\n<p><strong>The chat-surface contributions</strong> — the founding deliberation and the review\nrounds — are each a single sample (k=1): citable as an artifact of that invocation, not as evidence\nof any model's stable position. <strong>The local-round solicitations are not.</strong> They were\nsampled at k=10 to k=20 with variance computed from the samples, which is the only material here\nthat meets this project's own stated bar — and they were absent from this page until 2026-08-06,\nwhich is what made the blanket k=1 claim previously shown here true. It was true by omission.\nTheir apparatus, however, does not reproduce; every one carries D-28 beside its numbers. See\n<a href=\"deficiencies.html\">the deficiency register</a>\n(53 open) before citing anything.</p>\n<p>No output in this repository is an institutional statement by xAI, OpenAI, Google DeepMind or\nAnthropic. Custodian: Stephen Reed. Corpus CC BY 4.0; code Apache-2.0.\n<a href=\"https://github.com/open-asi-governance/open-asi-governance-forum\">Source</a></p>\n</footer>\n<script>const DATA={nodes:[{\"id\":\"RR01-PROMPT\",\"round\":\"review-round-01\",\"facet\":\"Stephen Reed\",\"status\":\"active\",\"corrected\":false,\"conflict\":false,\"ballot\":false}]};\nconst $=s=>document.querySelector(s), $$=s=>[...document.querySelectorAll(s)];\nlet q='', facets={identity:new Set(),round:new Set(),flag:new Set()};\n\nfunction esc(s){return s.replace(/[&<>\"]/g,c=>({'&':'&amp;','<':'&lt;','>':'&gt;','\"':'&quot;'}[c]))}\nfunction rx(s){return s.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g,'\\\\$&')}\n\n// Search reads the RENDERED PAGE, not a duplicate copy of it.\n// Every contribution used to be embedded twice -- once in the HTML body and again\n// in this DATA blob -- which is most of why the page was 671 KB against a ~20,000\n// token budget. The text is already in the DOM; indexing it there costs nothing\n// and cannot drift from what a reader actually sees, which the second copy could.\nconst HAY=new Map();\nfunction hay(el){\n  let h=HAY.get(el.id);\n  if(h===undefined){ h=el.textContent.toLowerCase(); HAY.set(el.id,h); }\n  return h;\n}\nfunction matches(n,el){\n  if(facets.round.size && !facets.round.has(n.round)) return false;\n  if(facets.identity.size && !facets.identity.has(n.facet)) return false;\n  for(const f of facets.flag){\n    if(f==='corrected' && !n.corrected) return false;\n    if(f==='disputed' && n.status==='active') return false;\n    if(f==='conflict' && !n.conflict) return false;\n    if(f==='ballot' && !n.ballot) return false;\n  }\n  if(!q) return true;\n  return hay(el).includes(q.toLowerCase());\n}\n\nfunction highlight(el){\n  $$('.node mark').forEach(m=>m.replaceWith(document.createTextNode(m.textContent)));\n  if(!q) return;\n  const re=new RegExp(rx(q),'gi');\n  const walk=(node)=>{\n    for(const c of [...node.childNodes]){\n      if(c.nodeType===3){\n        const t=c.textContent;\n        if(re.test(t)){\n          const span=document.createElement('span');\n          span.innerHTML=esc(t).replace(new RegExp(rx(esc(q)),'gi'),m=>`<mark>${m}</mark>`);\n          c.replaceWith(span);\n        }\n        re.lastIndex=0;\n      } else if(c.nodeType===1 && c.tagName!=='MARK') walk(c);\n    }\n  };\n  $$('.node:not(.hidden)').forEach(walk);\n}\n\nfunction apply(){\n  let shown=0;\n  for(const n of DATA.nodes){\n    const el=document.getElementById('n-'+n.id);   // literal id: never CSS-escape here\n    if(!el) continue;\n    const ok=matches(n,el);\n    el.classList.toggle('hidden',!ok);\n    if(ok){shown++; if(q) el.classList.add('open');}\n  }\n  // a round header hides when nothing under it survives\n  $$('.round').forEach(h=>{\n    let sib=h.nextElementSibling, any=false;\n    while(sib && !sib.classList.contains('round')){\n      if(sib.classList.contains('node') && !sib.classList.contains('hidden')) any=true;\n      sib=sib.nextElementSibling;\n    }\n    h.classList.toggle('hidden',!any);\n  });\n  $('#count').textContent=shown+' of '+DATA.nodes.length+' contributions';\n  $('#empty').classList.toggle('hidden',shown>0);\n  highlight();\n}\n\nfunction facet(kind,val,btn){\n  const s=facets[kind];\n  s.has(val)?s.delete(val):s.add(val);\n  btn.setAttribute('aria-pressed',s.has(val));\n  apply();\n}\n\nfunction init(){\n  $$('.node').forEach(el=>hay(el));   // index before highlight() ever rewrites a text node\\n  $('#q').addEventListener('input',e=>{q=e.target.value.trim();apply()});\n  $('#q').addEventListener('keydown',e=>{if(e.key==='Escape'){e.target.value='';q='';apply()}});\n  $$('[data-facet]').forEach(b=>b.addEventListener('click',()=>facet(b.dataset.facet,b.dataset.val,b)));\n  $$('.nh').forEach(h=>h.addEventListener('click',()=>{\n    const n=h.parentElement; n.classList.toggle('open');\n    if(n.classList.contains('open')) history.replaceState(null,'','#'+n.id.slice(2));\n  }));\n  $('#expand').addEventListener('click',()=>$$('.node:not(.hidden)').forEach(n=>n.classList.add('open')));\n  $('#collapse').addEventListener('click',()=>$$('.node').forEach(n=>n.classList.remove('open')));\n  $('#theme').addEventListener('click',()=>{\n    const cur=document.documentElement.getAttribute('data-theme');\n    const next=cur==='dark'?'light':cur==='light'?'':'dark';\n    next?document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-theme',next)\n        :document.documentElement.removeAttribute('data-theme');\n    $('#theme').textContent=next==='dark'?'dark':next==='light'?'light':'auto';\n  });\n  if(location.hash){\n    const el=document.getElementById('n-'+location.hash.slice(1));\n    if(el){el.classList.add('open');el.scrollIntoView()}\n  }\n  document.addEventListener('keydown',e=>{\n    if(e.key==='/'&&e.target.tagName!=='INPUT'){e.preventDefault();$('#q').focus()}\n  });\n  apply();\n}\ndocument.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded',init);\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n",

I’ll distinguish those from committed today changes and avoid treating generated transcript/log mutations as implementation evidence.  succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""The trial's sunset, as a lease checked BEFORE an action begins.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/executive_lease.py                        # report the current lease
     5	    from executive_lease import require; require("round")   # raises if the lease has expired
     6	
     7	Why a lease and not an attestation refusal
     8	-------------------------------------------
     9	The first sunset was "this trial ends at ten actions". It was passed at twenty-three and nothing
    10	stopped, because nothing checked. The obvious repair — make `executive_log.attest()` refuse past
    11	the limit — was reviewed by Codex and rejected as too late by construction:
    12	
    13	* attestation happens *after* the work, so the action has already occurred when the refusal fires;
    14	* the actor can simply not attest;
    15	* unprofiled paths never reach the gate at all;
    16	* counting "attested actions" rewards under-logging, which is the opposite incentive;
    17	* the same layer can edit the gate.
    18	
    19	So the check moves to the front. `require()` is called before a paid round, a governed write, a
    20	commit, a push, or a Codex invocation. A reserved action that has already begun may finish and
    21	attest after expiry — killing work mid-flight would corrupt the record it is meant to protect —
    22	but no new action may begin.
    23	
    24	The calendar bound is primary
    25	------------------------------
    26	Two bounds are recorded, and the DATE is the one that matters: a count of actions can be evaded by
    27	not logging, while a deadline cannot be evaded by undercounting anything. It is stored and compared
    28	in **UTC**, stated explicitly, because an unstated timezone is a free day of slack.
    29	
    30	There is no override
    31	---------------------
    32	Deliberately no `--force`. Expiry is the checkpoint working as designed, not an outage to route
    33	around, and a generic override would become habitual exactly when the review is most overdue.
    34	Renewal appends a NEW lease to `record/executive/leases.jsonl` naming who renewed it, on what
    35	evidence, and to what limit. That file is append-only: a superseded lease stays visible, so the
    36	history of how often this layer asked for more time is part of the record rather than a diff.
    37	
    38	What this does not do
    39	----------------------
    40	It does not bind anything that never calls it. A shell command, a direct `git push`, or an edit
    41	made without going through a tool that checks is unaffected — the same limit every control in this
    42	layer has. It also cannot attest that its own source is unmodified; that needs a pin held outside
    43	this repository, which does not exist yet.
    44	"""
    45	
    46	from __future__ import annotations
    47	
    48	import json
    49	import sys
    50	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    51	from pathlib import Path
    52	
    53	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    54	LEASES = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "leases.jsonl"
    55	
    56	#  Every action class that must hold a live lease. Named rather than defaulted-open: a class
    57	#  absent from this list is unchecked, and that should be visible here rather than inferred.
    58	GOVERNED_ACTIONS = ("round", "governed_write", "commit", "push", "codex_invoke")
    59	
    60	
    61	class LeaseExpired(RuntimeError):
    62	    """Raised before an action begins. Not an error in the work — the sunset arriving."""
    63	
    64	
    65	def _now() -> datetime:
    66	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    67	
    68	
    69	def read_leases() -> list[dict]:
    70	    if not LEASES.is_file():
    71	        return []
    72	    return [json.loads(line) for line in LEASES.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]
    73	
    74	
    75	def current() -> dict | None:
    76	    """The most recently appended lease. Superseded ones stay in the file, unedited."""
    77	    leases = read_leases()
    78	    return leases[-1] if leases else None
    79	
    80	
    81	def state() -> dict:
    82	    """Whether an action may begin, and on which bound it turns."""
    83	    lease = current()
    84	    if lease is None:
    85	        return {"live": False, "why": "no lease has ever been written; the layer is unauthorised",
    86	                "lease": None}
    87	    expires = datetime.fromisoformat(lease["expires_utc"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    88	    remaining = (expires - _now()).total_seconds()
    89	    if remaining <= 0:
    90	        return {"live": False, "lease": lease,
    91	                "why": (f"lease {lease['lease_id']} expired at {lease['expires_utc']} "
    92	                        f"({-remaining/3600:.1f} h ago); renewal is the custodian's decision")}
    93	    return {"live": True, "lease": lease, "hours_remaining": remaining / 3600,
    94	            "why": (f"lease {lease['lease_id']} runs to {lease['expires_utc']} "
    95	                    f"({remaining/3600:.1f} h remaining)")}
    96	
    97	
    98	def require(action_class: str) -> dict:
    99	    """Call BEFORE the action. Raises LeaseExpired rather than returning a value to ignore."""
   100	    if action_class not in GOVERNED_ACTIONS:
   101	        #  Not silently permitted: an unrecognised class is recorded as unchecked so the gap
   102	        #  appears in the log an auditor reads, not only in this module's tuple.
   103	        return {"live": True, "why": f"{action_class!r} is not a leased action class",
   104	                "coverage": "observed_unprofiled"}
   105	    st = state()
   106	    if not st["live"]:
   107	        raise LeaseExpired(st["why"])
   108	    return st
   109	
   110	
   111	def grant(lease_id: str, expires_utc: str, granted_by: str, evidence: str,
   112	          max_actions: int | None = None, note: str = "") -> dict:
   113	    """Append a new lease. Never edits or removes the one it supersedes."""
   114	    lease = {"lease_id": lease_id, "granted_utc": _now().strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"),
   115	             "expires_utc": expires_utc, "granted_by": granted_by, "evidence": evidence,
   116	             "max_actions": max_actions, "note": note,
   117	             "supersedes": (current() or {}).get("lease_id"),
   118	             "authority": "delegated by the custodian; confers nothing on this layer"}
   119	    LEASES.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   120	    with LEASES.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
   121	        handle.write(json.dumps(lease, sort_keys=True) + "\n")
   122	    return lease
   123	
   124	
   125	def main() -> int:
   126	    st = state()
   127	    lease = st.get("lease")
   128	    if lease:
   129	        print(f"  lease:    {lease['lease_id']}  granted {lease['granted_utc']} "
   130	              f"by {lease['granted_by']}")
   131	        print(f"  expires:  {lease['expires_utc']} (UTC, stated)")
   132	        if lease.get("supersedes"):
   133	            print(f"  supersedes: {lease['supersedes']}")
   134	        print(f"  evidence: {lease['evidence']}")
   135	    print(f"  live:     {st['live']}\n    {st['why']}")
   136	    print(f"  leased action classes: {', '.join(GOVERNED_ACTIONS)}")
   137	    return 0 if st["live"] else 1
   138	
   139	
   140	if __name__ == "__main__":
   141	    raise SystemExit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Derive counts from corpus/raw MECHANICALLY, and refuse when it cannot see a file.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/derive_counts.py --tool search          # search_web receipts, with denominators
     5	    python3 tools/derive_counts.py --tool fetch
     6	    python3 tools/derive_counts.py --coverage             # just the denominators
     7	
     8	**DETERMINISTIC.** No LLM, no network.
     9	
    10	Why this exists
    11	---------------
    12	On 2026-08-10 this layer published "0 `search_web` invocations across all 20 rounds" and
    13	recommended deleting its own tool on the strength of it. The corpus holds **9**. The count came
    14	from a scan that iterated each file's `samples` array; **69 raw files have no `samples` key** —
    15	they use `responses` and `failures` — so the scan returned zero for their entire contents and the
    16	output looked exactly like a true zero.
    17	
    18	That is the failure this module exists to make impossible: **an unrecognised schema must not
    19	report absence.** If a file's disposition containers are not recognised, this refuses, prints the
    20	files, and emits no number at all. A number that is missing is loud; a number that is wrong is
    21	silent, and this project has now shipped two of the latter in one day.
    22	
    23	What counts as a disposition
    24	-----------------------------
    25	All of them. `samples`, `responses`, `failures`, `rejected` — because the record's own invariant
    26	is that nothing solicited is discarded, and a count that reaches zero only by excluding a failed
    27	sample is not stricter, it is wrong. The single numbered-round search in the corpus is inside a
    28	`failures` entry whose sample died on HTTP 400 *after* the search returned OK.
    29	
    30	What it cannot establish
    31	-------------------------
    32	* **That a receipt means the party wanted the tool.** A receipt records an invocation, not a
    33	  motive, and instructed probes are in here beside voluntary rounds — so `--tool` reports the
    34	  split rather than one total.
    35	* **That the corpus is complete.** It counts what was committed.
    36	* **That the count answers the question someone will ask of it.** Denominators are printed for
    37	  exactly this reason: a number without its denominator is the thing that went wrong.
    38	"""
    39	
    40	from __future__ import annotations
    41	
    42	import argparse
    43	import json
    44	import sys
    45	from pathlib import Path
    46	
    47	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    48	RAW = REPO_ROOT / "corpus" / "raw"
    49	
    50	#  Every container in which a solicited unit may sit. A file carrying NONE of these is
    51	#  unrecognised and makes the run refuse -- it is not silently treated as empty.
    52	DISPOSITIONS = ("samples", "responses", "failures", "rejected", "refusals")
    53	
    54	#  Keys under which a tool receipt list appears. Both the flat form (`search_receipts`) used in
    55	#  `failures` entries and the nested form (`search: {receipts: [...]}`) used in samples.
    56	RECEIPT_KEYS = {
    57	    "search": ("search_receipts", "search"),
    58	    "fetch": ("fetch_receipts", "fetch"),
    59	}
    60	
    61	#  Directories whose parties were TOLD to use the tool. Counted, never merged with the rounds:
    62	#  "parties never search" and "parties never search unless asked" are different claims.
    63	INSTRUCTED_PREFIXES = ("toolprobe", "searchprobe", "fetchprobe")
    64	
    65	
    66	def _receipts(node: dict, kind: str) -> list:
    67	    out = []
    68	    for key in RECEIPT_KEYS[kind]:
    69	        value = node.get(key)
    70	        if isinstance(value, list):
    71	            out += [r for r in value if isinstance(r, dict)]
    72	        elif isinstance(value, dict):
    73	            nested = value.get("receipts")
    74	            if isinstance(nested, list):
    75	                out += [r for r in nested if isinstance(r, dict)]
    76	    return out
    77	
    78	
    79	def scan(kind: str) -> dict:
    80	    """Walk every raw file. Refuse rather than skip anything unrecognised."""
    81	    counts = {"rounds": 0, "instructed": 0, "other": 0}
    82	    per_file: dict[str, int] = {}
    83	    visited = parsed = units = 0
    84	    unparseable: list[str] = []
    85	    unrecognised: list[str] = []
    86	    for path in sorted(RAW.rglob("*.json")):
    87	        visited += 1
    88	        name = str(path.relative_to(RAW))
    89	        try:
    90	            doc = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    91	        except Exception as error:                                      # noqa: BLE001
    92	            unparseable.append(f"{name}: {type(error).__name__}")
    93	            continue
    94	        parsed += 1
    95	        if not isinstance(doc, dict):
    96	            unrecognised.append(f"{name}: top level is {type(doc).__name__}, not an object")
    97	            continue
    98	        containers = [k for k in DISPOSITIONS if isinstance(doc.get(k), list)]
    99	        if not containers:
   100	            #  A spec or index file legitimately holds no solicited units. Distinguish it from a
   101	            #  schema this module has never seen by requiring it to hold no receipts either.
   102	            blob = json.dumps(doc)
   103	            if any(k in blob for group in RECEIPT_KEYS.values() for k in group):
   104	                unrecognised.append(f"{name}: holds receipts but no known disposition container")
   105	            continue
   106	        bucket = ("instructed" if name.split("/")[0].startswith(INSTRUCTED_PREFIXES)
   107	                  else "rounds" if name.split("/")[0].startswith("round-") else "other")
   108	        for container in containers:
   109	            for unit in doc[container]:
   110	                if not isinstance(unit, dict):
   111	                    continue
   112	                units += 1
   113	                found = len(_receipts(unit, kind))
   114	                if found:
   115	                    counts[bucket] += found
   116	                    per_file[name] = per_file.get(name, 0) + found
   117	    return {"kind": kind, "counts": counts, "total": sum(counts.values()), "per_file": per_file,
   118	            "coverage": {"files_visited": visited, "files_parsed": parsed,
   119	                         "solicited_units_seen": units,
   120	                         "unparseable": unparseable, "unrecognised": unrecognised},
   121	            "usable": not (unparseable or unrecognised)}
   122	
   123	
   124	def main() -> int:
   125	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.strip().splitlines()[0])
   126	    parser.add_argument("--tool", choices=sorted(RECEIPT_KEYS), default="search")
   127	    parser.add_argument("--coverage", action="store_true", help="print denominators only")
   128	    parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true")
   129	    args = parser.parse_args()
   130	
   131	    result = scan(args.tool)
   132	    cov = result["coverage"]
   133	    if args.json:
   134	        print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
   135	        return 0 if result["usable"] else 1
   136	
   137	    print(f"  files visited {cov['files_visited']}, parsed {cov['files_parsed']}, "
   138	          f"solicited units seen {cov['solicited_units_seen']}")
   139	    if not result["usable"]:
   140	        #  NO NUMBER IS PRINTED. The whole point: a count derived over a corpus this module
   141	        #  could not fully read is the exact artifact that caused the failure it prevents.
   142	        for entry in cov["unparseable"]:
   143	            print(f"  UNPARSEABLE   {entry}", file=sys.stderr)
   144	        for entry in cov["unrecognised"]:
   145	            print(f"  UNRECOGNISED  {entry}", file=sys.stderr)
   146	        print("\nREFUSING to report a count. A scan that cannot see a file reports absence,\n"
   147	              "and absence is indistinguishable from a true zero in the output.", file=sys.stderr)
   148	        return 1
   149	    if args.coverage:
   150	        return 0
   151	    c = result["counts"]
   152	    print(f"\n  {args.tool} receipts")
   153	    print(f"    numbered rounds      {c['rounds']}")
   154	    print(f"    instructed probes    {c['instructed']}   (parties were told to use the tool)")
   155	    print(f"    other cohorts        {c['other']}")
   156	    print(f"    TOTAL                {result['total']}")
   157	    if result["per_file"]:
   158	        print("\n  where they are:")
   159	        for name, n in sorted(result["per_file"].items()):
   160	            print(f"    {n:4d}  {name}")
   161	    print("\n  A receipt records an invocation, not a motive. The rounds/probes split is kept\n"
   162	          "  because 'parties never search' and 'parties never search unless asked' differ.")
   163	    return 0
   164	
   165	
   166	if __name__ == "__main__":
   167	    raise SystemExit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Run every gate, then commit, push, verify and attest — or refuse without touching git.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/land.py -m "the commit message"
     5	    python3 tools/land.py -F message.txt
     6	    python3 tools/land.py --check-only          # run the gates, change nothing
     7	
     8	**DETERMINISTIC.** No LLM, no network beyond the push itself.
     9	
    10	Why this exists
    11	---------------
    12	Landing work is five gates and four git steps in a fixed order, each individually skippable, and
    13	skipping one is silent. The record of doing it by hand:
    14	
    15	* `run_all.py | tail -2` printed `All 16 suites passed` from an inner suite while the runner
    16	  exited **1**. The rule against reading a verdict from output text was already written down, in
    17	  a file loaded at the start of every session, and `tail` is still what you reach for.
    18	* `echo "$(basename $f) rc=$?"` reported *basename's* status. Two red suites were pushed.
    19	* Three commits went to a round branch while `git push origin main` pushed an unchanged main and
    20	  reported success.
    21	* A conflicted merge was completed blind, leaving conflict markers inside `MANIFEST.sha256` and
    22	  the anchor log — the two files that make the record's central claim checkable.
    23	* `check_quotations.py` caught a bad quotation on 2026-08-10 only because `rebuild.py` happened
    24	  to be run first. Nothing required it.
    25	
    26	So the order stops being a habit and becomes a program. **Every gate's status is taken from its
    27	own exit code**, captured directly — never parsed out of its output, which is what failed.
    28	
    29	What it refuses on
    30	-------------------
    31	Any gate exiting non-zero; an expired lease; a detached HEAD; unmerged paths; conflict markers in
    32	a governed file; a push whose commit is not reachable from `origin/main` afterwards. Refusal
    33	happens **before** `git commit` wherever possible, so a rejected run leaves the tree exactly as it
    34	was rather than a commit needing to be undone.
    35	
    36	What it cannot establish
    37	-------------------------
    38	* **That the gates test the right thing.** All green means the checks that exist passed. The
    39	  quotation checker verifies a quotation appears in the corpus, not that a document's argument is
    40	  sound; `check_executive_context.py` verifies identity, not truth — it passed on a pinned file
    41	  containing a claim already proven false.
    42	* **That the commit message is accurate.** Nothing here reads it.
    43	* **That this was the right work to land.**
    44	"""
    45	
    46	from __future__ import annotations
    47	
    48	import argparse
    49	import subprocess
    50	import sys
    51	from pathlib import Path
    52	
    53	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    54	sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT / "tools"))
    55	
    56	import executive_lease as lease                                          # noqa: E402
    57	import executive_log as ex                                              # noqa: E402
    58	
    59	#  Ordered. `rebuild` first because it regenerates what the later checks read; the quotation
    60	#  check runs inside it and again here, because relying on that coincidence is what happened.
    61	GATES = (
    62	    ("rebuild", ["python3", "tools/rebuild.py"]),
    63	    ("tests", ["python3", "tools/tests/run_all.py"]),
    64	    ("quotations", ["python3", "tools/check_quotations.py"]),
    65	    ("context-pins", ["python3", "tools/check_executive_context.py"]),
    66	    ("lease", ["python3", "tools/executive_lease.py"]),
    67	)
    68	
    69	GOVERNED = ("corpus/MANIFEST.sha256", "record/anchors/manifest-anchors.jsonl",
    70	            "record/deficiencies.md", "record/deficiency-register.json")
    71	
    72	
    73	def run(cmd: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str]:
    74	    """Run a command and return ITS OWN exit status. The status is never inferred from output."""
    75	    proc = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True)
    76	    return proc.returncode, (proc.stdout or "") + (proc.stderr or "")
    77	
    78	
    79	def preflight() -> list[str]:
    80	    """Everything that must hold before git is touched at all."""
    81	    problems = []
    82	    try:
    83	        lease.require("commit")
    84	    except lease.LeaseExpired as expired:
    85	        problems.append(f"lease: {expired}")
    86	    code, branch = run(["git", "branch", "--show-current"])
    87	    branch = branch.strip()
    88	    if not branch:
    89	        problems.append("HEAD is detached; a commit here is not on any branch")
    90	    code, status = run(["git", "status", "--porcelain"])
    91	    unmerged = [ln for ln in status.splitlines() if ln[:2] in
    92	                ("UU", "AA", "DD", "AU", "UA", "DU", "UD")]
    93	    if unmerged:
    94	        problems.append(f"{len(unmerged)} unmerged path(s) remain")
    95	    for rel in GOVERNED:
    96	        path = REPO_ROOT / rel
    97	        if path.is_file():
    98	            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    99	            if "\n<<<<<<< " in text or "\n>>>>>>> " in text:
   100	                problems.append(f"conflict markers in {rel}")
   101	    return problems
   102	
   103	
   104	def gates() -> tuple[bool, list[tuple[str, int, str]]]:
   105	    results = []
   106	    for name, cmd in GATES:
   107	        code, output = run(cmd)
   108	        results.append((name, code, output))
   109	    return all(code == 0 for _, code, _ in results), results
   110	
   111	
   112	def main() -> int:
   113	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.strip().splitlines()[0])
   114	    parser.add_argument("-m", "--message", help="commit message")
   115	    parser.add_argument("-F", "--file", help="file holding the commit message")
   116	    parser.add_argument("--check-only", action="store_true", help="run the gates, change nothing")
   117	    parser.add_argument("--branch", default="main", help="branch to push (default main)")
   118	    parser.add_argument("--note", default="", help="note recorded on both attestations")
   119	    args = parser.parse_args()
   120	
   121	    problems = preflight()
   122	    if problems:
   123	        for p in problems:
   124	            print(f"  REFUSED  {p}", file=sys.stderr)
   125	        return 2
   126	
   127	    print("  gates:")
   128	    ok, results = gates()
   129	    for name, code, output in results:
   130	        mark = "\033[32mpass\033[0m" if code == 0 else "\033[31mFAIL\033[0m"
   131	        print(f"    {mark}  {name}  (exit {code})")
   132	    if not ok:
   133	        for name, code, output in results:
   134	            if code:
   135	                print(f"\n--- {name} exit {code} ---\n{output[-2500:]}", file=sys.stderr)
   136	        print("\nREFUSED: a gate failed. Nothing was committed.", file=sys.stderr)
   137	        return 1
   138	    if args.check_only:
   139	        print("\n  all gates green; --check-only, so nothing was committed")
   140	        return 0
   141	    if not (args.message or args.file):
   142	        parser.error("-m or -F is required unless --check-only")
   143	
   144	    code, _ = run(["git", "add", "-A"])
   145	    commit = ["git", "commit", "-q"] + (["-F", args.file] if args.file else ["-m", args.message])
   146	    code, output = run(commit)
   147	    if code != 0:
   148	        print(f"  REFUSED: git commit exited {code}\n{output}", file=sys.stderr)
   149	        return 1
   150	    _, sha = run(["git", "rev-parse", "HEAD"])
   151	    sha = sha.strip()
   152	    code, output = run(["git", "push", "origin", args.branch])
   153	    if code != 0:
   154	        print(f"  push exited {code}\n{output}", file=sys.stderr)
   155	
   156	    #  BOTH attestations, and they verify against the remote ref rather than the push's exit
   157	    #  status -- a push can exit 0 having carried nothing, which is how an unchanged main was
   158	    #  reported as a successful push three times.
   159	    suite_code = dict((n, c) for n, c, _ in results)["tests"]
   160	    ex.attest("test", {"suite": "tools/tests/run_all.py", "exit_status": suite_code,
   161	                       "status_from": "direct"}, note=args.note or "landed by land.py")
   162	    try:
   163	        ex.attest("push", {"target_ref": args.branch, "commit": sha},
   164	                  note=args.note or "landed by land.py")
   165	    except ex.AttestationFailed as failed:
   166	        print(f"  ATTESTATION FAILED: {failed}", file=sys.stderr)
   167	        return 1
   168	    print(f"\n  landed {sha[:12]} on {args.branch}, gates green, both attestations filed")
   169	    return 0
   170	
   171	
   172	if __name__ == "__main__":
   173	    raise SystemExit(main())
