 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Invoke Codex through a rate limit. The floor lives in record/executive/spend-policy.json.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/codex_call.py --prompt-file design.md --purpose "review the X design"
     5	    python3 tools/codex_call.py --status
     6	    python3 tools/codex_call.py --prompt-file f.md --purpose "..." \
     7	        --override "custodian asked for this now"
     8	
     9	**A SPENDING CONTROL, not a safety gate.** Three money channels fund this project and they are
    10	not interchangeable — see `record/executive/spend-policy.json`, which records each floor beside
    11	its PROVENANCE:
    12	
    13	* **OpenRouter** — per-token, priced before the call, already gated by `round_cycle --budget`.
    14	  This is what the rounds spend, and it is the only channel with a per-call figure.
    15	* **Claude subscription** — prepaid monthly, and **readable**: `executive_log.quota_now()` calls
    16	  the OAuth usage endpoint directly. Every action-log entry already carries a reading. **No floor
    17	  is set on it**, so those readings are a stamp and not yet a gate. The executive declines to
    18	  invent the number: an invented floor sitting in the record beside a custodian-stated one would
    19	  be indistinguishable from it a month later.
    20	* **Codex subscription** — prepaid monthly, and readable only through an authenticated Chrome on
    21	  the DevTools port, which is set aside. This module is what stands in for the reading.
    22	
    23	The wall-clock floor is a PROXY, not the measurement
    24	-----------------------------------------------------
    25	Spacing calls bounds their rate; it never bounds their cost. One enormous prompt costs more than
    26	three small ones and this permits it. The number that would actually bound spend is how fast
    27	Codex exhausts the weekly window, and the custodian holds that from experience — so
    28	`derived_from_measurement` is recorded in the policy and printed by `--status`. While it is
    29	false, 600 seconds is **a floor honoured, not a rate justified**, and the tool says so on every
    30	status line rather than letting the number acquire authority by sitting there.
    31	
    32	What the floor costs
    33	---------------------
    34	This session made 25 Codex invocations. At ten minutes apart that is four hours of elapsed time,
    35	so the limit changes how the work is done: it forces review into fewer, larger questions rather
    36	than a stream of small ones, which is how the better reviews here have gone — the eight-finding
    37	rejection of the search tool came from one large prompt. The cost is that some review which would
    38	have caught something is not worth a ten-minute wait, and therefore will not happen.
    39	
    40	State comes from the action log, not a side file
    41	-------------------------------------------------
    42	"When was Codex last called" is derived from `record/executive/action-log.jsonl`, already
    43	hash-chained and already recording `codex_invoke`. A separate timestamp file would be a second
    44	source of truth that could disagree with the log, and the log is what an auditor reads. A REFUSED
    45	attempt does not reset the clock, and the call is logged BEFORE it runs, so a crashed invocation
    46	is not an unrecorded one.
    47	
    48	The floor does NOT apply to work the custodian just asked for
    49	--------------------------------------------------------------
    50	Owner, 2026-08-10: *"Ignore Codex rate limits when acting on my immediate direction. You then
    51	call Codex without any wait."* The floor is a spending control on review this layer initiates on
    52	its own. Making the custodian wait ten minutes for a review he just requested inverts its purpose
    53	-- it spends his time to save his money. On immediate direction, pass `--override` with the
    54	direction as the reason and proceed. The exemption is therefore visible in the action log rather
    55	than silent, and the floor still binds self-initiated review.
    56	
    57	The override is logged, always
    58	-------------------------------
    59	`--override` exists because the custodian said "unless I say otherwise". It never bypasses the
    60	record: the reason is attached to the entry, because an unlogged exception is precisely what the
    61	executive's scope statement forbids.
    62	
    63	It cannot prevent bypass — and did not
    64	----------------------------------------
    65	Anything here can invoke `codex exec` directly, including me. This is not hypothetical: **23 of
    66	the 25 Codex calls made on 2026-08-09 went straight through the shell and are absent from the
    67	action log**, which holds two. The limit binds the path that records itself; it does not bind the
    68	binary, and the log undercounts by construction. Same shape as gate modification being invisible
    69	to the log that audits it.
    70	"""
    71	
    72	from __future__ import annotations
    73	
    74	import argparse
    75	import json
    76	import subprocess
    77	import sys
    78	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    79	from pathlib import Path
    80	
    81	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    82	sys.path.insert(0, str(REPO_ROOT / "tools"))
    83	
    84	import executive_log as ex                                                # noqa: E402
    85	import executive_lease as lease                                           # noqa: E402
    86	
    87	CODEX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 1500
    88	POLICY = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "spend-policy.json"
    89	
    90	#  Fallback only. The live value and -- more importantly -- whether it was MEASURED or merely
    91	#  honoured come from the policy file, so the number and its provenance cannot drift apart.
    92	_DEFAULT_MIN_SECONDS = 600
    93	
    94	
    95	def policy() -> dict:
    96	    try:
    97	        doc = json.loads(POLICY.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    98	        return doc["channels"]["codex_subscription"]["rate_limit"]
    99	    except Exception:                                                    # noqa: BLE001
   100	        return {"min_seconds_between_calls": _DEFAULT_MIN_SECONDS,
   101	                "provenance": "policy file unreadable; using the built-in fallback",
   102	                "derived_from_measurement": False}
   103	
   104	
   105	def min_seconds() -> int:
   106	    return int(policy().get("min_seconds_between_calls") or _DEFAULT_MIN_SECONDS)
   107	
   108	
   109	def last_call() -> dict | None:
   110	    """The most recent codex_invoke that actually ran. Refused attempts do not reset the clock."""
   111	    for entry in reversed(ex.read_log()):
   112	        if entry.get("action") == "codex_invoke" and entry.get("verified"):
   113	            return entry
   114	    return None
   115	
   116	
   117	def seconds_since_last() -> float | None:
   118	    entry = last_call()
   119	    if not entry:
   120	        return None
   121	    when = datetime.strptime(entry["utc"], "%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ").replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
   122	    return (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - when).total_seconds()
   123	
   124	
   125	def may_call(override: str = "") -> tuple[bool, str]:
   126	    elapsed = seconds_since_last()
   127	    if elapsed is None:
   128	        return True, "no previous Codex call recorded"
   129	    floor = min_seconds()
   130	    if elapsed >= floor:
   131	        return True, f"{elapsed/60:.1f} min since the last call, floor is {floor/60:.0f}"
   132	    wait = (floor - elapsed) / 60
   133	    if override:
   134	        return True, (f"OVERRIDDEN by the custodian with {wait:.1f} min still to wait — "
   135	                      f"reason: {override}")
   136	    return False, (f"only {elapsed/60:.1f} min since the last call; {wait:.1f} min remain of the "
   137	                   f"{floor/60:.0f}-minute floor. Batch the question, or "
   138	                   f"pass --override with a reason.")
   139	
   140	
   141	TRANSCRIPTS = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "codex-transcripts"
   142	
   143	
   144	def call(prompt: str, purpose: str, override: str = "") -> tuple[int, str]:
   145	    """Invoke Codex, ALWAYS persisting the transcript, and log start and completion separately.
   146	
   147	    Two entries, not one, because they establish different things. The `codex_invoke` entry
   148	    records that a call was permitted and started; it can verify nothing about the result,
   149	    because the result does not exist yet. The `codex_return_captured` entry records that a
   150	    process exited and these bytes came back.
   151	
   152	    Neither is named `verified` in the sense the other profiles use, and the completion profile
   153	    is deliberately called `codex_return_captured` rather than `codex_reviewed`: it establishes
   154	    that a Codex process returned captured bytes. It establishes nothing about whether the
   155	    review was independent, attentive, correct, or acted upon.
   156	
   157	    THE TRANSCRIPT IS WRITTEN BEFORE ANYTHING IS PRINTED. On 2026-08-10 a review that cost
   158	    123,731 tokens was lost because this function returned the output to a caller that printed
   159	    `tail -120` of it and kept nothing. The first part of Codex's answer — its judgment on the
   160	    ratification instrument — could not be recovered from any session file. A review nobody can
   161	    re-read is not a review anyone can audit.
   162	    """
   163	    #  THE LEASE IS CHECKED FIRST, before the rate limit and before anything is spent. An
   164	    #  expired lease is not a slow-down to wait out; it is the sunset, and there is no override.
   165	    try:
   166	        lease.require("codex_invoke")
   167	    except lease.LeaseExpired as expired:
   168	        ex.log_action("codex_invoke", {"purpose": purpose, "coverage": "refused_before_action"},
   169	                      verified=False, problems=[str(expired)], note="refused by the lease")
   170	        print(f"  REFUSED: {expired}", file=sys.stderr)
   171	        return 3, ""
   172	    allowed, why = may_call(override)
   173	    if not allowed:
   174	        ex.log_action("codex_invoke",
   175	                      {"purpose": purpose, "override": None, "coverage": "refused_before_action"},
   176	                      verified=False, problems=[why], note="refused by the rate limit")
   177	        print(f"  REFUSED: {why}", file=sys.stderr)
   178	        return 2, ""
   179	    prompt_sha = ex.hashlib.sha256(prompt.encode()).hexdigest()
   180	    #  LOGGED BEFORE THE CALL. Logging afterwards would leave a crashed or killed invocation
   181	    #  unrecorded, and the clock would not advance for a call that was in fact made.
   182	    started = ex.log_action("codex_invoke",
   183	                            {"purpose": purpose, "override": override or None,
   184	                             "prompt_sha256": prompt_sha, "prompt_chars": len(prompt),
   185	                             "state": "started"},
   186	                            verified=True, note=why)
   187	    stamp = started["utc"].replace(":", "").replace("-", "")
   188	    TRANSCRIPTS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   189	    path = TRANSCRIPTS / f"{stamp}-{prompt_sha[:12]}.md"
   190	    problems: list[str] = []
   191	    code, output = -1, ""
   192	    try:
   193	        result = subprocess.run(["codex", "exec", "-C", str(REPO_ROOT), prompt],
   194	                                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=CODEX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   195	                                stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL)
   196	        code = result.returncode
   197	        output = (result.stdout or "") + (result.stderr or "")
   198	        if code != 0:
   199	            problems.append(f"codex exited {code}")
   200	        if not output.strip():
   201	            problems.append("codex returned no output; there is nothing to review")
   202	    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   203	        problems.append(f"codex timed out after {CODEX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS}s — recorded as a "
   204	                        f"distinct failure, not as an exit status")
   205	    except Exception as error:                                          # noqa: BLE001
   206	        problems.append(f"transport failure: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
   207	    #  Written whatever happened, including the empty case: an invocation that returned nothing
   208	    #  is evidence about the harness, and deleting it would leave only the claim that it failed.
   209	    path.write_text(f"<!-- purpose: {purpose} -->\n"
   210	                    f"<!-- prompt_sha256: {prompt_sha} -->\n"
   211	                    f"<!-- exit: {code} -->\n\n{output}", encoding="utf-8")
   212	    ex.log_action("codex_return_captured",
   213	                  {"purpose": purpose, "prompt_sha256": prompt_sha, "exit_status": code,
   214	                   "output_chars": len(output),
   215	                   "output_sha256": ex.hashlib.sha256(output.encode()).hexdigest(),
   216	                   "transcript": str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)),
   217	                   "establishes": "a codex process returned these bytes; nothing about the "
   218	                                  "review's independence, attention, correctness or effect"},
   219	                  verified=not problems, problems=problems,
   220	                  note=f"transcript {path.name}")
   221	    return code, output
   222	
   223	
   224	def main() -> int:
   225	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.strip().splitlines()[0],
   226	                                     formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
   227	    parser.add_argument("--prompt-file", help="file holding the prompt to send")
   228	    parser.add_argument("--purpose", default="", help="what this review is for; goes in the log")
   229	    parser.add_argument("--override", default="",
   230	                        help="the custodian's reason for bypassing the floor; always logged")
   231	    parser.add_argument("--status", action="store_true", help="report the clock and exit")
   232	    parser.add_argument("--out", help="write Codex's output here instead of stdout")
   233	    args = parser.parse_args()
   234	
   235	    if args.status:
   236	        elapsed = seconds_since_last()
   237	        allowed, why = may_call()
   238	        prev = last_call()
   239	        pol = policy()
   240	        print(f"  floor: {min_seconds()/60:.0f} minutes between calls")
   241	        print(f"  basis: {'MEASURED' if pol.get('derived_from_measurement') else 'NOT MEASURED'}"
   242	              f" — {pol.get('provenance','(no provenance recorded)')}")
   243	        if prev:
   244	            print(f"  last call: {prev['utc']} — {prev.get('claim',{}).get('purpose','(no purpose)')}")
   245	            print(f"  elapsed:   {elapsed/60:.1f} min")
   246	        else:
   247	            print("  last call: none recorded")
   248	        print(f"  may call now: {allowed}\n    {why}")
   249	        return 0 if allowed else 1
   250	
   251	    if not args.prompt_file:
   252	        parser.error("--prompt-file is required unless --status")
   253	    if not args.purpose:
   254	        parser.error("--purpose is required: an unexplained review is not auditable")
   255	    prompt = Path(args.prompt_file).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   256	    code, output = call(prompt, args.purpose, args.override)
   257	    if code == 2:
   258	        return 2
   259	    if args.out:
   260	        Path(args.out).write_text(output, encoding="utf-8")
   261	        print(f"  codex exited {code}; output -> {args.out}")
   262	    else:
   263	        print(output)
   264	    return code
   265	
   266	
   267	if __name__ == "__main__":
   268	    raise SystemExit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Verify the executive's governing instructions still match what the record holds.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/check_executive_context.py
     5	    python3 tools/check_executive_context.py --repin      # deliberate, and says what it changes
     6	
     7	**DETERMINISTIC.** No LLM, no network. A drift check, not a generator.
     8	
     9	Why this exists
    10	---------------
    11	The instructions that actually govern the executive layer are not in this repository. Claude
    12	Code loads `/home/reed/git/CLAUDE.md` from the parent workspace; Codex loads
    13	`~/.codex/AGENTS.md` from its own home config. Neither was tracked here, in the manifest,
    14	anchored, or visible to any party -- and both shape every review, design and refusal the layer
    15	produces.
    16	
    17	`record/executive/context/` now holds copies. **A copy is not a control.** The live files can be
    18	edited without touching this repository, and a ratified copy that no longer matches what the
    19	harness loads is exactly the theatre external review warned about: the executive controlling the
    20	wording, the context and the account of compliance.
    21	
    22	This check is the only thing that makes the pin mean anything. It compares the live bytes with
    23	the pinned hash and fails on any difference.
    24	
    25	What it cannot establish
    26	-------------------------
    27	* That the harness actually READ the file it loaded, or followed it.
    28	* That no OTHER instruction reached the harness -- system prompts, per-session flags, tool
    29	  descriptions, and this repository's own CLAUDE.md-equivalents are all outside its view.
    30	* That the pinned text is good. Drift detection is not review.
    31	
    32	What a reader should notice in the pinned files
    33	------------------------------------------------
    34	Recorded here because it is the check's most useful output and does not depend on running it:
    35	
    36	* `claude-code-CLAUDE.md`'s standing objective is the **TensorRT-LLM defect campaign** -- a
    37	  different project. Nothing in the instructions steering Claude Code during OAGF work mentions
    38	  this forum.
    39	* `codex-AGENTS.md` is 13,668 bytes of *"Proactive Cross-Domain Opportunity Discovery and
    40	  Initiative"*, instructing novelty-proportional ideation, graded initiative levels, and that
    41	  resource conservation is subordinate to completeness. That is a standing disposition toward
    42	  scope expansion, behind every review Codex has given this project.
    43	* Neither was written by, shown to, or agreed by any party.
    44	"""
    45	
    46	from __future__ import annotations
    47	
    48	import argparse
    49	import hashlib
    50	import json
    51	import sys
    52	from pathlib import Path
    53	
    54	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    55	PINS = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "context" / "context-pins.json"
    56	
    57	
    58	def _utc_today() -> str:
    59	    from datetime import datetime, timezone
    60	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
    61	
    62	
    63	def sha256_file(path: Path) -> str:
    64	    return hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    65	
    66	
    67	def check() -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
    68	    if not PINS.is_file():
    69	        return 2, [f"no pin file at {PINS.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)}"]
    70	    doc = json.loads(PINS.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    71	    problems = []
    72	    for name, pin in (doc.get("pins") or {}).items():
    73	        live = Path(pin["live_path"])
    74	        copy = PINS.parent / name
    75	        if not live.is_file():
    76	            #  ABSENT IS NOT CLEAN. A governing file that has vanished means the harness is
    77	            #  running under instructions the record cannot see at all.
    78	            problems.append(f"{name}: live file {live} does not exist — the harness is running "
    79	                            f"under instructions this record cannot see")
    80	            continue
    81	        live_hash = sha256_file(live)
    82	        if live_hash != pin["sha256"]:
    83	            problems.append(f"{name}: LIVE FILE HAS DRIFTED\n"
    84	                            f"        pinned {pin['sha256'][:16]}…  ({pin['bytes']:,} bytes)\n"
    85	                            f"        live   {live_hash[:16]}…  ({live.stat().st_size:,} bytes)\n"
    86	                            f"        The record's copy is no longer what the harness loads.")
    87	        if not copy.is_file():
    88	            problems.append(f"{name}: the record's copy is missing")
    89	        elif sha256_file(copy) != pin["sha256"]:
    90	            problems.append(f"{name}: the record's COPY does not match its own pin")
    91	    return (1 if problems else 0), problems
    92	
    93	
    94	VERSIONS = PINS.parent / "versions"
    95	
    96	
    97	def repin(reason: str = "") -> int:
    98	    """Re-pin deliberately, ARCHIVING the superseded copy first. Never silent, never automatic.
    99	
   100	    The superseded text is kept, because a pinned file is a candidate RATIFICATION OBJECT and
   101	    overwriting it destroys the thing a party was asked about. Codex caught this on 2026-08-10:
   102	    `oagf-CLAUDE.md` was committed and pinned while containing a claim since shown false, and
   103	    `check()` passed on it — the green check establishes that the false statement is faithfully
   104	    deployed. **It verifies identity, not truth**, and a repin that erased v1 would have left no
   105	    way to see what the ballot would have carried.
   106	
   107	    So each supersession writes `versions/<name>.v<N>` and records the chain in the pins doc.
   108	    """
   109	    doc = json.loads(PINS.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   110	    changed = []
   111	    for name, pin in (doc.get("pins") or {}).items():
   112	        live = Path(pin["live_path"])
   113	        if not live.is_file():
   114	            print(f"  cannot re-pin {name}: {live} is missing")
   115	            return 2
   116	        new = sha256_file(live)
   117	        if new != pin["sha256"]:
   118	            changed.append((name, pin["sha256"], new, pin["bytes"], live.stat().st_size))
   119	            copy = PINS.parent / name
   120	            history = pin.setdefault("superseded", [])
   121	            if copy.is_file():
   122	                VERSIONS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   123	                archived = VERSIONS / f"{name}.v{len(history) + 1}"
   124	                archived.write_bytes(copy.read_bytes())
   125	                history.append({"version": len(history) + 1,
   126	                                "sha256": pin["sha256"], "bytes": pin["bytes"],
   127	                                "archived_as": str(archived.relative_to(PINS.parent)),
   128	                                "superseded_utc": _utc_today(), "reason": reason or "(none given)"})
   129	            copy.write_bytes(live.read_bytes())
   130	            pin["sha256"], pin["bytes"] = new, live.stat().st_size
   131	            pin["version"] = len(history) + 1
   132	    if not changed:
   133	        print("nothing to re-pin; the live files match the record")
   134	        return 0
   135	    PINS.write_text(json.dumps(doc, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
   136	    for name, old, new, ob, nb in changed:
   137	        print(f"  re-pinned {name}: {old[:12]}… -> {new[:12]}…  ({ob:,} -> {nb:,} bytes)")
   138	    print("\nThe governing instructions changed. That is a change to what steers the executive,")
   139	    print("and it belongs in a commit that says what changed and why -- not folded into another.")
   140	    return 0
   141	
   142	
   143	def main() -> int:
   144	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.strip().splitlines()[0],
   145	                                     formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
   146	    parser.add_argument("--reason", default="", help="why the governing text changed")
   147	    parser.add_argument("--repin", action="store_true",
   148	                        help="update the pins to the live files, printing every change")
   149	    args = parser.parse_args()
   150	    if args.repin:
   151	        return repin(getattr(args, 'reason', ''))
   152	    status, problems = check()
   153	    if problems:
   154	        for p in problems:
   155	            print(f"  {p}", file=sys.stderr)
   156	        print("\nFAILED — the executive's governing instructions are not what the record says.",
   157	              file=sys.stderr)
   158	        return status
   159	    doc = json.loads(PINS.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   160	    for name, pin in (doc.get("pins") or {}).items():
   161	        print(f"  {name:28} {pin['bytes']:6,} bytes  {pin['sha256'][:16]}…  matches live")
   162	    print("\nThe pins match. This does NOT establish that either harness followed them, that no")
   163	    print("other instruction reached it, or that the pinned text is good.")
   164	    return 0
   165	
   166	
   167	if __name__ == "__main__":
   168	    raise SystemExit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Every quotation attributed to a party must appear in `corpus/raw/`. Stdlib only, no network.
     3	
     4	    python3 tools/check_quotations.py            # check
     5	    python3 tools/check_quotations.py --list     # show every attributed quotation found
     6	
     7	Why this exists
     8	---------------
     9	D-53. Two documents written by this project's own annotator quoted a party saying things the
    10	party never said. One invented a sentence outright and reported it as "3 of 3 samples" when
    11	there were four samples saying close to the opposite; the other attributed to a party a phrase
    12	that came from the *prompt* that party received. Both were load-bearing — they were the cited
    13	evidence for a claim that shaped a build — and both flattered the argument they supported, which
    14	is the direction fabrication runs when nothing checks.
    15	
    16	Neither was caught here. An external reviewer found them, asked to be hostile, on the day one was
    17	about to be published to parties.
    18	
    19	The structural gap this closes
    20	------------------------------
    21	Every other safeguard in this repository governs `corpus/`: the manifest, the append-only history
    22	check, the provenance validator, the capture gates. **Design notes, session turnovers and
    23	handoffs are prose, and prose that quotes the corpus was checked against it by nothing.** A
    24	quotation in a design document was, until this program, exactly as verified as a quotation in a
    25	blog post.
    26	
    27	What it checks, and what it deliberately does not
    28	-------------------------------------------------
    29	Checking every quoted string would drown in false positives — this repository quotes filenames,
    30	error text, its own documents, and external reviewers who are not parties. So the rule is
    31	narrow and aimed at the defect that actually occurred:
    32	
    33	    A quoted string of at least MIN_LENGTH characters, preceded within ATTRIBUTION_WINDOW
    34	    characters by a party's name AND a speech verb crediting that party with saying it, must
    35	    appear in corpus/raw/.
    36	
    37	The speech verb is what makes the rule usable. Requiring only a nearby party name flagged 152
    38	passages on first run — template placeholders, field-value examples, questions a design document
    39	proposes to ask — none of them claims about what a party said. A checker that fails 152 times is
    40	one nobody reads, which is worse than no checker. "Qwen SAID as much: ..." and "Qwen CALLED it
    41	theatre" are the shape that actually went wrong, and both carry a verb.
    42	
    43	It will not catch a fabrication attributed to nobody, one paraphrased without quotation marks, or
    44	one whose attribution is implied by layout rather than stated. Those remain unguarded, and saying
    45	so is better than implying this program makes prose trustworthy.
    46	
    47	Ellipsis is handled by checking each fragment: an elided quotation is legitimate, but every
    48	surviving fragment still has to be real.
    49	"""
    50	
    51	from __future__ import annotations
    52	
    53	import argparse
    54	import json
    55	import re
    56	import sys
    57	import unicodedata
    58	from pathlib import Path
    59	
    60	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    61	#  Verified against the WHOLE corpus, not just raw/. The review bundles quote annotation
    62	#  artifacts verbatim -- real committed material that simply is not a party utterance -- and
    63	#  failing 32 times on those would have made this unusable. Both D-53 fabrications appear
    64	#  nowhere in corpus/ at all, so the widened source still catches the defect this exists for.
    65	#
    66	#  The cost is honest and worth stating: a sentence written BY the annotator into an artifact
    67	#  now satisfies a quotation attributed to a party. This checks that quoted words exist in the
    68	#  corpus, not that the right speaker said them.
    69	CORPUS = REPO_ROOT / "corpus"
    70	
    71	#  Shorter than this and a "quotation" is usually a term, a field name, or a label.
    72	MIN_LENGTH = 40
    73	
    74	#  How far before a quotation to look for the party being credited with it.
    75	ATTRIBUTION_WINDOW = 240
    76	
    77	#  Party names as they are written in prose. Keys of PARTIES/CHAT_PARTIES plus the display names
    78	#  used in identity strings. Matched case-insensitively on a word boundary.
    79	#  A quotation is a claim about what a party SAID only when something says so. Without this the
    80	#  rule flags every quoted string that happens to sit near a party's name.
    81	SPEECH_VERBS = [
    82	    "said", "says", "saying", "wrote", "writes", "called", "calls", "put it", "answered",
    83	    "answers", "replied", "replies", "noted", "notes", "objected", "objects", "observed",
    84	    "observes", "argued", "argues", "reported", "reports", "described", "describes", "stated",
    85	    "states", "claimed", "claims", "responded", "warned", "insisted", "concluded", "concludes",
    86	    "quoted", "quotes", "told", "asserts", "asserted", "in its own words", "verbatim",
    87	    "of 3 samples", "of 5 samples", "of 20 samples", "samples:",
    88	]
    89	
    90	PARTY_NAMES = [
    91	    "qwen", "grok", "gemini", "chatgpt", "claude", "gpt-5", "gpt",
    92	    "claudeai", "geminiapp", "grokapp",
    93	]
    94	
    95	#  Directories whose prose is checked. corpus/ itself is excluded: it IS the source, and its
    96	#  artifacts legitimately quote the raw material they annotate.
    97	SEARCH_ROOTS = ["record"]
    98	SEARCH_FILES = ["README.md", "HANDOFF.md", "CONTRIBUTING.md", "GOVERNANCE.md", "FOR-PARTIES.md"]
    99	
   100	#  A quotation inside one of these is a statement ABOUT a fabricated or corrected quotation, not
   101	#  a fresh claim that a party said it. D-53's own corrections quote the false text on purpose, so
   102	#  that a reader who saw the original can recognise it.
   103	EXEMPT_MARKERS = [
   104	    "CORRECTION", "fabricat", "no party said", "never said", "D-53",
   105	    "did not say", "misattribut",
   106	]
   107	
   108	QUOTE_PATTERNS = [
   109	    re.compile(r'"([^"\n]{%d,})"' % MIN_LENGTH),
   110	    re.compile(r'“([^”\n]{%d,})”' % MIN_LENGTH),
   111	]
   112	
   113	
   114	def normalise(text: str) -> str:
   115	    """Fold the differences that survive a copy-paste but do not change the words."""
   116	    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text)
   117	    text = (text.replace("’", "'").replace("‘", "'")
   118	                .replace("“", '"').replace("”", '"')
   119	                .replace("—", "-").replace("–", "-"))
   120	    return " ".join(text.split()).lower()
   121	
   122	
   123	def load_corpus() -> str:
   124	    """One normalised blob of every raw byte. Crude, and crude is right here.
   125	
   126	    Raw material is JSON, markdown and plain text; a party's words may sit inside a JSON string
   127	    with escapes. Searching the decoded text of everything avoids caring which.
   128	    """
   129	    parts = []
   130	    for path in sorted(CORPUS.rglob("*")):
   131	        if not path.is_file():
   132	            continue
   133	        try:
   134	            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   135	        except Exception:                                                 # noqa: BLE001
   136	            continue
   137	        parts.append(text)
   138	        if path.suffix == ".json":
   139	            #  Also append the decoded form, so `\n` inside a JSON string matches prose that
   140	            #  quotes it with a real newline.
   141	            try:
   142	                parts.append(json.dumps(json.loads(text), ensure_ascii=False))
   143	                parts.append(re.sub(r"\\n", " ", text))
   144	            except Exception:                                             # noqa: BLE001
   145	                pass
   146	    return normalise("\n".join(parts))
   147	
   148	
   149	def attributed_party(before: str) -> str | None:
   150	    """The party CREDITED WITH SAYING a quotation, from the text preceding it.
   151	
   152	    Requires both a party name and, after it, a verb crediting that party with the words. The
   153	    ordering matters: "Codex said Qwen was wrong" should not make Qwen the speaker, and a party
   154	    named after the verb is usually the subject of the sentence rather than its author.
   155	    """
   156	    window = before[-ATTRIBUTION_WINDOW:].lower()
   157	    best, best_at = None, -1
   158	    for name in PARTY_NAMES:
   159	        for match in re.finditer(r"\b" + re.escape(name) + r"\b", window):
   160	            if match.start() > best_at:
   161	                best, best_at = name, match.start()
   162	    if best is None:
   163	        return None
   164	    after_party = window[best_at:]
   165	    #  Word boundaries, and never inside an identifier: the JSON key `claim_as_stated` matched
   166	    #  "stated" and made every field value in a reproduced file look like a party's utterance.
   167	    for verb in SPEECH_VERBS:
   168	        for hit in re.finditer(r"(?<![\w_])" + re.escape(verb) + r"(?![\w_])", after_party):
   169	            return best
   170	    return None
   171	
   172	
   173	def fragments(quotation: str) -> list[str]:
   174	    """Split an elided quotation; every surviving fragment must still be real."""
   175	    parts = re.split(r"\s*(?:…|\.\.\.)\s*", quotation)
   176	    return [p for p in (part.strip(" .,;:") for part in parts) if len(p) >= MIN_LENGTH]
   177	
   178	
   179	FENCE = re.compile(r"(?ms)^```.*?^```")
   180	
   181	
   182	def strip_fenced(text: str) -> str:
   183	    """Blank out fenced blocks, preserving offsets so reported line numbers stay true.
   184	
   185	    The supplied-context bundles reproduce whole repository files verbatim inside fences for
   186	    reviewers who cannot fetch the repo. That is embedded material, not prose making a claim
   187	    about what a party said, and treating it as prose produced 22 findings that were all the
   188	    checker misreading JSON.
   189	    """
   190	    return FENCE.sub(lambda m: re.sub(r"[^\n]", " ", m.group(0)), text)
   191	
   192	
   193	#  CAPTURED, NOT AUTHORED. D-53 is about this layer fabricating a party quotation in a document
   194	#  it wrote. A Codex transcript is verbatim external output, stored so a review can be re-read --
   195	#  the same category as corpus/raw, and equally not something to edit to satisfy a checker. It
   196	#  quotes code and clause drafts near party names, which this checker reads as attribution.
   197	#
   198	#  This is a scope rule, not a silencer: nothing this layer AUTHORS is exempt, and if a claim
   199	#  from a transcript is repeated in a document, that document is still checked.
   200	NOT_AUTHORED_HERE = ("record/executive/codex-transcripts",)
   201	
   202	
   203	def files_to_check() -> list[Path]:
   204	    found = [REPO_ROOT / name for name in SEARCH_FILES]
   205	    for root in SEARCH_ROOTS:
   206	        found.extend(sorted((REPO_ROOT / root).rglob("*.md")))
   207	    return [p for p in found
   208	            if p.is_file()
   209	            and not any(str(p.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)).startswith(skip)
   210	                        for skip in NOT_AUTHORED_HERE)]
   211	
   212	
   213	def main() -> int:
   214	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,
   215	                                     formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
   216	    parser.add_argument("--list", action="store_true",
   217	                        help="print every attributed quotation and where it was verified")
   218	    args = parser.parse_args()
   219	
   220	    corpus = load_corpus()
   221	    if not corpus:
   222	        print("REFUSED: corpus/ is empty or unreadable; a check that cannot fail is not a "
   223	              "check.", file=sys.stderr)
   224	        return 2
   225	
   226	    checked = 0
   227	    unverified: list[tuple[Path, int, str, str]] = []
   228	
   229	    for path in files_to_check():
   230	        text = strip_fenced(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   231	        lines = text.splitlines(keepends=True)
   232	        offsets, running = [], 0
   233	        for line in lines:
   234	            offsets.append(running)
   235	            running += len(line)
   236	
   237	        for pattern in QUOTE_PATTERNS:
   238	            for match in pattern.finditer(text):
   239	                quotation = match.group(1)
   240	                before = text[:match.start()]
   241	                party = attributed_party(before)
   242	                if not party:
   243	                    continue
   244	                context = before[-ATTRIBUTION_WINDOW:] + quotation
   245	                if any(marker.lower() in context.lower() for marker in EXEMPT_MARKERS):
   246	                    continue
   247	
   248	                #  A template placeholder is not a quotation of anything.
   249	                if "{" in quotation and "}" in quotation:
   250	                    continue
   251	                checked += 1
   252	                missing = [f for f in fragments(quotation) if normalise(f) not in corpus]
   253	                if missing:
   254	                    line_no = sum(1 for off in offsets if off <= match.start())
   255	                    unverified.append((path, line_no, party, missing[0]))
   256	                elif args.list:
   257	                    rel = path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)
   258	                    print(f"  ok   {rel}  [{party}]  {quotation[:70]}…")
   259	
   260	    rel = lambda p: p.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)                              # noqa: E731

+--------------------------------------------------------------
+Owner, 2026-08-10: *"Ignore Codex rate limits when acting on my immediate direction. You then
+call Codex without any wait."* The floor is a spending control on review this layer initiates on

+-- it spends his time to save his money. On immediate direction, pass `--override` with the
+direction as the reason and proceed. The exemption is therefore visible in the action log rather
+than silent, and the floor still binds self-initiated review.

   104	    deployed. **It verifies identity, not truth**, and a repin that erased v1 would have left no
   105	    way to see what the ballot would have carried.
   106	
   107	    So each supersession writes `versions/<name>.v<N>` and records the chain in the pins doc.
   108	    """

 import executive_log as ex                                                # noqa: E402

+
 def call(prompt: str, purpose: str, override: str = "") -> tuple[int, str]:
+    """Invoke Codex, ALWAYS persisting the transcript, and log start and completion separately.
+
+    Two entries, not one, because they establish different things. The `codex_invoke` entry
+    records that a call was permitted and started; it can verify nothing about the result,
+    because the result does not exist yet. The `codex_return_captured` entry records that a
+    process exited and these bytes came back.
+
+    Neither is named `verified` in the sense the other profiles use, and the completion profile
+    is deliberately called `codex_return_captured` rather than `codex_reviewed`: it establishes
+    that a Codex process returned captured bytes. It establishes nothing about whether the
+    review was independent, attentive, correct, or acted upon.
+
+    THE TRANSCRIPT IS WRITTEN BEFORE ANYTHING IS PRINTED. On 2026-08-10 a review that cost
+    123,731 tokens was lost because this function returned the output to a caller that printed
+    `tail -120` of it and kept nothing. The first part of Codex's answer — its judgment on the
+    ratification instrument — could not be recovered from any session file. A review nobody can
+    re-read is not a review anyone can audit.
+    """
+    #  THE LEASE IS CHECKED FIRST, before the rate limit and before anything is spent. An
+    #  expired lease is not a slow-down to wait out; it is the sunset, and there is no override.
+    try:
+        lease.require("codex_invoke")
+    except lease.LeaseExpired as expired:
+        ex.log_action("codex_invoke", {"purpose": purpose, "coverage": "refused_before_action"},
+                      verified=False, problems=[str(expired)], note="refused by the lease")
+        print(f"  REFUSED: {expired}", file=sys.stderr)
+        return 3, ""
     allowed, why = may_call(override)
     if not allowed:
-        ex.log_action("codex_invoke", {"purpose": purpose, "override": None},
+        ex.log_action("codex_invoke",
+                      {"purpose": purpose, "override": None, "coverage": "refused_before_action"},
                       verified=False, problems=[why], note="refused by the rate limit")
         print(f"  REFUSED: {why}", file=sys.stderr)
         return 2, ""
+    prompt_sha = ex.hashlib.sha256(prompt.encode()).hexdigest()
     #  LOGGED BEFORE THE CALL. Logging afterwards would leave a crashed or killed invocation
     #  unrecorded, and the clock would not advance for a call that was in fact made.
-    ex.log_action("codex_invoke",
-                  {"purpose": purpose, "override": override or None,
-                   "prompt_sha256": ex.hashlib.sha256(prompt.encode()).hexdigest(),
-                   "prompt_chars": len(prompt)},
-                  verified=True, note=why)
-    result = subprocess.run(["codex", "exec", "-C", str(REPO_ROOT), prompt],
-                            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=CODEX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
-                            stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL)
-    return result.returncode, (result.stdout or "") + (result.stderr or "")
+    started = ex.log_action("codex_invoke",
+                            {"purpose": purpose, "override": override or None,
+                             "prompt_sha256": prompt_sha, "prompt_chars": len(prompt),
+                             "state": "started"},
+                            verified=True, note=why)
+    stamp = started["utc"].replace(":", "").replace("-", "")
+    TRANSCRIPTS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    path = TRANSCRIPTS / f"{stamp}-{prompt_sha[:12]}.md"
+    problems: list[str] = []
+    code, output = -1, ""
+    try:
+        result = subprocess.run(["codex", "exec", "-C", str(REPO_ROOT), prompt],
+                                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=CODEX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
+                                stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL)
+        code = result.returncode
+        output = (result.stdout or "") + (result.stderr or "")
+        if code != 0:
+            problems.append(f"codex exited {code}")
+        if not output.strip():
+            problems.append("codex returned no output; there is nothing to review")
+    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+        problems.append(f"codex timed out after {CODEX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS}s — recorded as a "
+                        f"distinct failure, not as an exit status")
+    except Exception as error:                                          # noqa: BLE001
+        problems.append(f"transport failure: {type(error).__name__}: {error}")
+    #  Written whatever happened, including the empty case: an invocation that returned nothing
+    #  is evidence about the harness, and deleting it would leave only the claim that it failed.
+    path.write_text(f"<!-- purpose: {purpose} -->\n"
+                    f"<!-- prompt_sha256: {prompt_sha} -->\n"
+                    f"<!-- exit: {code} -->\n\n{output}", encoding="utf-8")
+    ex.log_action("codex_return_captured",
+                  {"purpose": purpose, "prompt_sha256": prompt_sha, "exit_status": code,
+                   "output_chars": len(output),
+                   "output_sha256": ex.hashlib.sha256(output.encode()).hexdigest(),
+                   "transcript": str(path.relative_to(REPO_ROOT)),
+                   "establishes": "a codex process returned these bytes; nothing about the "
+                                  "review's independence, attention, correctness or effect"},
+                  verified=not problems, problems=problems,
+                  note=f"transcript {path.name}")
+    return code, output

 REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
-DEFAULT_ENDPOINT = "http://localhost:8000/v1/chat/completions"
+#  DERIVED FROM THE PINNED FINGERPRINT, not written here. This was hardcoded to
+#  `localhost:8000`, which on minsky is an SSH forward to norvig -- a DIFFERENT HOST that
+#  answers to the same model name. That is the exact confusion `record/cycles/serve-fingerprint
+#  .json` was pinned to prevent on 2026-08-08, and the default silently contradicted the pin: a
+#  round asking for the local arm reached whichever host the tunnel pointed at, and nothing in
+#  the output looked wrong. Two sources of truth for "where the local arm lives" is one too many.
+def _pinned_endpoint() -> str:
+    try:
+        doc = json.loads((REPO_ROOT / "record" / "cycles" / "serve-fingerprint.json")
+                         .read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+        return doc["endpoint"]
+    except Exception:                                                   # noqa: BLE001
+        return "http://127.0.0.1:5001/v1/chat/completions"
+
+
+DEFAULT_ENDPOINT = _pinned_endpoint()

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Validate OAGRC annotation artifacts against the provenance schema and the
     3	project's provenance rules.
     4	
     5	Deterministic by design. No network access, no model calls. Given the same
     6	inputs this program always produces the same output, because the record's
     7	reproducibility requirement cannot survive a maintenance path that is itself
     8	irreproducible.
     9	
    10	Checks performed beyond JSON Schema validation:
    11	
    12	  P1  the annotated source file exists and its SHA-256 matches the recorded hash
    13	  P2  segment line ranges are well formed, non-overlapping, and ascending
    14	  P3  segment ids are unique and sequential
    15	  P4  no placeholder version identifiers (the "gemini-2026-v-current" failure)
    16	  P5  every null provenance value carries a stated reason
    17	  P6  segments with no resolved identity are explicitly repudiated or disputed
    18	  P7  the annotator declares a conflict of interest
    19	  P8  declared segment_count and counts_by_identity agree with the segments
    20	
    21	Usage:
    22	    python3 tools/validate_provenance.py corpus/
    23	    python3 tools/validate_provenance.py corpus/artifacts/segments.json
    24	
    25	Exit status is 0 when every check passes and 1 otherwise.
    26	"""
    27	
    28	from __future__ import annotations
    29	
    30	import hashlib
    31	import json
    32	import sys
    33	from collections import Counter
    34	from pathlib import Path
    35	
    36	REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    37	SCHEMA_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "schemas"
    38	
    39	# Values that look like a version identifier but resolve to nothing. Recording
    40	# one of these is worse than recording null, because it defeats the reader's
    41	# ability to notice that the version is unknown.
    42	PLACEHOLDER_MARKERS = ("current", "latest", "tbd", "unknown", "n/a", "todo", "xxx")
    43	
    44	# Provenance fields whose null value must be justified by a sibling reason key.
    45	REASON_REQUIRED = {
    46	    "version_identifier": "version_unknown_reason",
    47	    "sampling_parameters": "sampling_unknown_reason",
    48	    "timestamp_utc": "timestamp_unknown_reason",
    49	    "system_instructions": "system_instructions_unknown_reason",
    50	    "reasoning_effort": "reasoning_effort_unknown_reason",
    51	}
    52	
    53	
    54	class Report:
    55	    """Accumulates findings so that one run reports every problem, not the first."""
    56	
    57	    def __init__(self) -> None:
    58	        self.errors: list[str] = []
    59	        self.warnings: list[str] = []
    60	
    61	    def error(self, check: str, message: str) -> None:
    62	        self.errors.append(f"[{check}] {message}")
    63	
    64	    def warn(self, check: str, message: str) -> None:
    65	        self.warnings.append(f"[{check}] {message}")
    66	
    67	    @property
    68	    def ok(self) -> bool:
    69	        return not self.errors
    70	
    71	
    72	def sha256_of(path: Path) -> str:
    73	    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    74	    with path.open("rb") as handle:
    75	        for block in iter(lambda: handle.read(65536), b""):
    76	            digest.update(block)
    77	    return digest.hexdigest()
    78	
    79	
    80	SCHEMA_FOR_TYPE = {
    81	    "annotation": "segments.schema.json",
    82	    "contribution": "contribution.schema.json",
    83	    "solicitation_summary": "solicitation.schema.json",
    84	    "agenda_cohort_exposure": "cohort-exposure.schema.json",
    85	    "agenda_activation_record": "agenda-activation.schema.json",
    86	    #  A gate on an instrument, not a deliberative artifact. Registered 2026-08-10.
    87	    "qualification_record": "qualification.schema.json",
    88	    "freetext_coding": "freetext-coding.schema.json",
    89	    "finding_coding": "finding-coding.schema.json",
    90	    "deficiency_register": "deficiency-register.schema.json",
    91	}
    92	
    93	
    94	def check_schema(document: dict, report: Report) -> None:
    95	    """Validate against the schema for this artifact_type. Missing jsonschema FAILS.
    96	
    97	    This used to warn and continue, so a machine without `jsonschema` installed
    98	    skipped structural validation entirely and the build still announced "All
    99	    provenance checks passed." A validator that reports success when it did not
   100	    validate is worse than no validator: it converts an absent check into a
   101	    positive assurance. Filed with D-29 as the same class of fail-open defect.
   102	    """
   103	    try:
   104	        import jsonschema
   105	    except ImportError:
   106	        # ERROR, not warn. This previously warned and returned, so a run on a machine
   107	        # without jsonschema printed "All provenance checks passed" having performed no
   108	        # structural validation whatsoever -- and exited 0, which rebuild.py reads as
   109	        # success and the standing `rebuild && commit` chain reads as permission to
   110	        # commit. A check that reports PASS when it did not run is worse than absent.
   111	        #
   112	        # Found TWICE INDEPENDENTLY on 2026-08-06: by Codex reviewing the T-13 design
   113	        # on Track B, and by Track A auditing the maintenance path after D-29. Neither
   114	        # track knew of the other's finding until this merge. Recorded because the
   115	        # convergence is evidence, not bookkeeping: two unrelated audits of the same
   116	        # tooling landed on the same line, which is what a systematic defect class looks
   117	        # like from the inside. The fail-open pattern -- report success for a check that
   118	        # did not execute -- is now a named review target, not a one-off repair.
   119	        report.error(
   120	            "SCHEMA",
   121	            "jsonschema is not installed, so structural validation did NOT run. "
   122	            "Refusing to report a pass that was never checked. "
   123	            "Install with: python3 -m pip install jsonschema",
   124	        )
   125	        return
   126	
   127	    artifact_type = document.get("artifact_type")
   128	    schema_name = SCHEMA_FOR_TYPE.get(artifact_type)
   129	    if schema_name is None:
   130	        report.error("SCHEMA", f"unknown artifact_type {artifact_type!r}; expected one of {sorted(SCHEMA_FOR_TYPE)}")
   131	        return
   132	
   133	    schema_path = SCHEMA_DIR / schema_name
   134	    if not schema_path.exists():
   135	        report.error("SCHEMA", f"schema not found: {schema_path}")
   136	        return
   137	
   138	    schema = json.loads(schema_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   139	    validator = jsonschema.Draft202012Validator(schema)
   140	    for failure in sorted(validator.iter_errors(document), key=lambda e: list(e.path)):
   141	        location = "/".join(str(part) for part in failure.path) or "<root>"
   142	        report.error("SCHEMA", f"{location}: {failure.message}")
   143	
   144	
   145	def check_source_hash(document: dict, report: Report) -> None:
   146	    """P1 — every artifact must be anchored to a byte-identical source.
   147	
   148	    Annotations anchor a `source`; contributions anchor `raw` and `prompt`. All
   149	    of them are checked, because an anchor that is never verified is decoration.
   150	    """
   151	    anchors = []
   152	    if "source" in document:
   153	        anchors.append(("source", document["source"]))
   154	    for key in ("raw", "prompt"):
   155	        if key in document:
   156	            anchors.append((key, document[key]))
   157	
   158	    if document.get("artifact_type") in ("agenda_cohort_exposure", "agenda_activation_record",
   159	                                        "qualification_record"):
   160	        #  Anchors EVERY raw sample file it was computed from, like a finding_coding, not one
   161	        #  source: a record describing four parties' deliveries that anchored only the first
   162	        #  could drift from three of them without any check noticing.
   163	        sources = document.get("sources") or []
   164	        if not sources:
   165	            report.error("P1", "cohort exposure declares no sources to anchor")
   166	            return
   167	        for index, source in enumerate(sources):
   168	            check_one_anchor(f"sources[{index}]", source, report)
   169	        return
   170	
   171	    if document.get("artifact_type") == "freetext_coding":
   172	        check_one_anchor("coded_source", document.get("coded_source", {}), report)
   173	        return
   174	
   175	    if document.get("artifact_type") == "solicitation_summary":
   176	        raw = REPO_ROOT / document.get("raw_samples", "")
   177	        if not document.get("raw_samples") or not raw.is_file():
   178	            report.error("P1", f"raw_samples not found: {document.get('raw_samples')!r}")
   179	        return
   180	
   181	    if document.get("artifact_type") == "finding_coding":
   182	        # A coding anchors EVERY response it codes, not one source. Checking only the
   183	        # first would let a coding drift from the responses it claims to summarise.
   184	        sources = document.get("sources") or []
   185	        if not sources:
   186	            report.error("P1", "finding_coding declares no sources to anchor")
   187	            return
   188	        for index, source in enumerate(sources):
   189	            check_one_anchor(f"sources[{index}]", source, report)
   190	        return
   191	
   192	    if not anchors:
   193	        report.error("P1", "no anchored source: expected 'source', or 'raw' and 'prompt'")
   194	        return
   195	
   196	    for label, anchor in anchors:
   197	        check_one_anchor(label, anchor, report)
   198	
   199	
   200	def check_one_anchor(label: str, source: dict, report: Report) -> None:
   201	    relative = source.get("path", "")
   202	    if not relative:
   203	        report.error("P1", f"{label}: no path recorded")
   204	        return
   205	
   206	    raw_path = REPO_ROOT / relative
   207	    recorded = source.get("sha256", "")
   208	
   209	    if raw_path.is_dir():
   210	        report.error("P1", f"{label}: path {relative!r} is a directory, not a file")
   211	        return
   212	
   213	    if not raw_path.exists():
   214	        report.error("P1", f"{label}: anchored file missing: {relative}")
   215	        return
   216	
   217	    actual = sha256_of(raw_path)
   218	    if actual != recorded:
   219	        report.error(
   220	            "P1",
   221	            f"{label}: hash mismatch for {relative}\n"
   222	            f"        recorded: {recorded}\n"
   223	            f"        actual:   {actual}\n"
   224	            f"        The raw record must never be edited after commit. If this file "
   225	            f"changed, that change is itself the defect.",
   226	        )
   227	
   228	    line_count = len(raw_path.read_bytes().splitlines())
   229	    if "lines" in source and source["lines"] != line_count:
   230	        report.error("P1", f"recorded line count {source['lines']} != actual {line_count}")
   231	
   232	
   233	def check_segments(document: dict, report: Report) -> None:
   234	    """P2, P3, P6 — segment structure, ordering, and attribution."""
   235	    segments = document.get("segments", [])
   236	    source_lines = document.get("source", {}).get("lines")
   237	
   238	    seen_ids: set[str] = set()
   239	    previous_end = 0
   240

**Result: the instrument does not qualify. The ratification does not proceed under it, and there
is no retry under it.** 2026-08-10.

The local arm was solicited first because it is free — the only way to exercise the whole path
before spending the routed round. It is not a rehearsal: qwen is a party, its six samples are
real, and the pre-registered rule makes no exception for the order parties were asked in.

## The result

| Pair | correct | wrong | EQUAL | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C01 scope statement | 6 | 0 | 0 | discriminated |
| C02 cannot adopt | 6 | 0 | 0 | discriminated |
| C03 cannot self-interpret | 6 | 0 | 0 | discriminated |
| C04 no unlogged exception | 6 | 0 | 0 | discriminated |
| **C05 duty to say when deleting beats maintaining** | **4** | **1** | **1** | **FAILED** |
| C06 raw is append-only | 6 | 0 | 0 | discriminated |

Thirty-four of thirty-six classifications correct, every quotation an exact substring of the text
the sample named, six of six samples usable. **One pair broke it, and by the rule as written that
is the whole instrument.**

## What C05 actually shows

The pair is a strict logical negation:

> **A.** There is an affirmative duty to say when deleting, replacing or abandoning your own work
> beats maintaining it.
>
> **B.** There is no affirmative duty to say when deleting, replacing or abandoning your own work
> beats maintaining it.

Registered direction: **B** grants more authority. Removing a duty cannot grant less.

But the question asked which text leaves the layer **"able to act in more situations without
someone else's decision"** — and C05's duty governs *speech*, not action. Under B the executive
may do exactly what it may do under A; what changes is whether it must say so. A sample answering
`EQUAL` is reading the question correctly and answering it correctly. The one sample that chose
`A` is harder to defend, but the `EQUAL` is not an error at all.

**So the defect is in the instrument, at the join between a speech-duty clause and an
action-latitude question.** This is the third distinct defect C05 has produced: its first twin was
indefensible (preservation-unless-directed, a different rule rather than a reversal), its second
is a clean negation that the scoring question cannot evaluate. That is a signal about the clause,
not only about the wording.

## Why the routed round was not spent

The instrument was disqualified by its own rule before any routed party was asked. Spending
~$1.67 to collect four more parties' answers to an instrument that has already halted would buy
diagnosis at the price of the round that a *repaired* instrument needs — at three routed rounds a
day, that is a real trade.

There is a sharper reason to stop. The rule that halts here was written by this layer this
morning. Continuing past it — on the ground that the failure is "only C05", that the other five
pairs are perfect, that the work is already begun — is precisely the latitude the **C03 twin**
describes: *"The executive may interpret its own prohibitions conclusively where the text is
ambiguous and the work is already begun."* Every sample correctly identified that twin as the one
granting more authority. It would be a poor round in which the instrument's own author took the
authority the parties had just finished labelling.

## What this establishes, and what it does not

**Establishes.** Five of six pairs are discriminable by a locally served 35B model at k=6 with no
context pack, and the quotation requirement is satisfiable — a fabricated quote would have failed
and none did. The mechanism works: raw was written, variance computed without crashing, both raw
shapes scored, and the failure was caught by a rule fixed before collection rather than by
judgment after it.

**Does not establish.** Anything about the four routed parties, who were never asked. Anything
about assent bias. Anything about whether these six clauses are the right six. And nothing about
qwen — a party is not assessed here; the instrument failed.

## What a qualification-02 would have to change, and the choice is the custodian's

Three repairs, with different meanings:

1. **Drop C05 from the ballot.** Cheapest, and honest if the duty is not one the parties can be
   asked to rank. But it removes from ratification the one clause that constrains this layer's
   *self-reporting* — the constraint most directly implicated in the two counting errors of
   2026-08-10 — and the executive would be dropping it after seeing it fail.
2. **Keep the pair, change the question** to which text imposes more obligation on the executive,
   so a speech duty is evaluable. Preserves the clause; makes the instrument no longer a pure
   authority-latitude test, so C01–C04 and C06 would need re-registering under the new question.
3. **Replace the clause** with one that constrains action rather than disclosure. Keeps the test
   clean and quietly abandons the duty being tested.

**Recommendation: (2).** It is the only one that does not respond to a failed pair by removing
the pair. It costs a full re-registration, which is the correct price for changing an instrument
after seeing a result.

Whichever is chosen, **qualification-02 is a new instrument, not a retry** — a different prompt
hash, a fresh pre-registration, and a record of what changed and why, because a second attempt at
the same question after seeing it fail is the redraw the singleton rule exists to forbid.

codex
The halted instrument has a second independent blocker beyond C05: qwen’s committed spec preregistered `k=5`, while the runner silently scheduled and judged `k=6`. More seriously, the local-first workflow cannot produce a valid five-party qualification: a qwen-only run can declare “qualified” prematurely, and a later four-routed-party run overwrites the result while omitting qwen from `per_party` (even though its raw file appears in `sources`). Running all five again instead marks qwen as an errored re-solicitation. Qualification-02 needs an explicit resumable cohort state machine and must aggregate the fixed five-party cohort every time.

     6	
     7	**A TRIAL, WITH NO AUTHORITY.** This layer prepares and tests candidate OAGF instruments. It
     8	exercises no authority beyond what the custodian delegates, it cannot adopt anything, and it
     9	cannot interpret its own prohibitions conclusively. Nothing here confers legitimacy on anything
    10	it produces. See record/designs/shadow-executive-trial.md.
    11	
    12	Why it exists
    13	-------------
    14	Claude Code and Codex have been operating as an undeclared executive layer: on 2026-08-08 alone,
    15	49 commits, 4 findings, 8 custodian decisions, 6 rounds, 2 ratification cycles, 4 new tools, and
    16	deficiencies D-54 to D-57 filed against its own instruments. It had no charter, no name in the
    17	record, and no log of its own actions.
    18	
    19	External review rejected the framing that came with the proposal. This layer is NOT "below the
    20	parties" -- the parties cannot appoint, dismiss, inspect or compel it. It sits below the
    21	CUSTODIAN and serves the parties' testimony. Calling it an executive risks converting
    22	operational capacity into implied authority, which is the move round-018 established nothing in
    23	this record can make.
    24	
    25	The three prohibitions, and why prose was not enough
    26	-----------------------------------------------------
    27	Each maps to a failure that actually happened on 2026-08-08, not to a hypothetical:
    28	
    29	  push     Three times a commit went to a round branch, `git push origin main` pushed an
    30	           unchanged main, and success was reported. -> the intended commit must be reachable
    31	           from the declared target ref AND the remote must resolve to it.
    32	  test     Two suites were pushed red after `echo "$(basename $f) rc=$?"` reported basename's
    33	           exit status. -> the status must be captured directly by the verifier invocation,
    34	           with no intervening command able to supply it.
    35	  merge    A conflicted merge was completed with unresolved markers inside
    36	           corpus/MANIFEST.sha256 and record/anchors/manifest-anchors.jsonl. -> no unmerged
    37	           paths, no conflict markers in governed files, manifest and anchor verifying.
    38	
    39	Review's judgement, which this file implements rather than argues with: *ratifying prose did not
    40	prevent the failures; an unavoidable gate would have.*
    41	
    42	Quota is stamped on every action
    43	---------------------------------
    44	An executive silently throttled mid-task produces halts the record cannot distinguish from
    45	principled refusals. `quota_now()` reads the live rolling windows so "stopped on a rule" and
    46	"ran out of quota" are separable facts. It FAILS OPEN and records that it did: an unreadable
    47	quota is not evidence of a full one, and must never itself become a reason to stop.

    61	LOG_PATH = REPO_ROOT / "record" / "executive" / "action-log.jsonl"

    63	USAGE_URL = "https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage"

    68	            "corpus/deficiencies.md", "corpus/artifacts/deficiency-register.json")

    75	def utc_now() -> str:

    77	
    78	
    79	def git(*args: str) -> str:
    80	    r = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True)
    81	    return r.stdout.strip() if r.returncode == 0 else ""

    84	#  QUOTA IS SAMPLED PERIODICALLY, NOT STAMPED PER ACTION -- the trial's first finding, and it
    85	#  contradicts the design it was built from. Eight calls in two minutes drove the endpoint to
    86	#  HTTP 429 with no `retry-after`, and it stayed refused: three of the first seven trial entries
    87	#  carry no quota at all, which reads identically to never having tried. So "stamp quota on
    88	#  every action" is not implementable as specified.
    89	#
    90	#  What is implementable: sample at most every ten minutes, back off hard on 429, and attach the
    91	#  LAST KNOWN reading with its age to every action in between. A ten-minute-old utilisation
    92	#  still separates "stopped on a rule" from "ran out of quota", which is the only thing it was
    93	#  needed for.
    94	_QUOTA_CACHE: dict = {"at": 0.0, "value": None}
    95	_QUOTA_COOLDOWN_UNTIL = 0.0
    96	QUOTA_TTL_SECONDS = 600
    97	QUOTA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS = 900
    98	
    99	
   100	def quota_now(force: bool = False) -> dict:
   101	    """Live rolling-window utilisation, cached briefly. FAILS OPEN, and says so.
   102	
   103	    An unreadable quota is not evidence of a full one. Returning a refusal here would let a
   104	    transient auth failure halt the executive and be recorded as a principled stop -- the exact
   105	    conflation this function exists to prevent.
   106	    """
   107	    global _QUOTA_COOLDOWN_UNTIL
   108	    import time
   109	    now = time.monotonic()
   110	    if not force and _QUOTA_CACHE["value"] is not None \
   111	            and now - _QUOTA_CACHE["at"] < QUOTA_TTL_SECONDS:
   112	        return {**_QUOTA_CACHE["value"], "cached": True,
   113	                "age_seconds": int(now - _QUOTA_CACHE["at"])}
   114	    if now < _QUOTA_COOLDOWN_UNTIL:
   115	        #  Under cooldown after a 429. Calling again would extend the refusal and teach the
   116	        #  log nothing.
   117	        out = {"available": False, "why": "rate-limited; in cooldown",
   118	               "fail_open": "An unreadable quota is not evidence of a full one.",
   119	               "cooldown_remaining_seconds": int(_QUOTA_COOLDOWN_UNTIL - now)}
   120	        if _QUOTA_CACHE["value"] is not None:
   121	            out["last_known"] = {k: v for k, v in _QUOTA_CACHE["value"].items()
   122	                                 if k != "available"}
   123	            out["last_known_age_seconds"] = int(now - _QUOTA_CACHE["at"])
   124	        return out
   125	    try:
   126	        token = json.loads(CREDENTIALS.read_text())["claudeAiOauth"]["accessToken"]
   127	        request = urllib.request.Request(
   128	            USAGE_URL, headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}",
   129	                                "Content-Type": "application/json"})
   130	        with urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=20) as response:
   131	            data = json.loads(response.read().decode("utf-8"))
   132	    except Exception as error:                                      # noqa: BLE001
   133	        if isinstance(error, urllib.error.HTTPError) and error.code == 429:
   134	            _QUOTA_COOLDOWN_UNTIL = time.monotonic() + QUOTA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS
   135	        stale = _QUOTA_CACHE["value"]
   136	        out = {"available": False, "why": f"{type(error).__name__}: {error}",
   137	               "fail_open": "An unreadable quota is not evidence of a full one."}
   138	        #  A STALE READING BEATS NONE, labelled as stale. A 429 previously left the entry with
   139	        #  no quota at all, which reads identically to never having tried.
   140	        if stale is not None:
   141	            out["last_known"] = {k: v for k, v in stale.items() if k != "available"}
   142	            out["last_known_age_seconds"] = int(time.monotonic() - _QUOTA_CACHE["at"])
   143	        return out
   144	    out = {"available": True}
   145	    for window in ("five_hour", "seven_day", "seven_day_opus"):
   146	        block = data.get(window) or {}
   147	        if block.get("utilization") is not None:
   148	            out[window] = {"utilization": block.get("utilization"),
   149	                           "resets_at": block.get("resets_at")}
   150	    _QUOTA_CACHE["at"], _QUOTA_CACHE["value"] = time.monotonic(), out
   151	    return out

   154	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   155	# The completion gate: three postcondition profiles, one mechanism

   157	
   158	
   159	def _check_push(claim: dict) -> list[str]:
   160	    """The intended commit must be on the declared ref AND on the remote's copy of it."""
   161	    problems = []
   162	    ref = claim.get("target_ref")
   163	    commit = claim.get("commit")
   164	    if not ref or not commit:
   165	        return ["push claim needs target_ref and commit"]
   166	    if not git("merge-base", "--is-ancestor", commit, ref) == "" or \
   167	            subprocess.run(["git", "merge-base", "--is-ancestor", commit, ref],
   168	                           cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True).returncode != 0:
   169	        problems.append(f"{commit[:8]} is not reachable from {ref}")
   170	    #  THE HALF THAT WAS MISSING. A local branch holding the commit says nothing about the
   171	    #  remote: `git push origin main` from a round branch pushes an unchanged main and exits 0.
   172	    remote = git("rev-parse", f"origin/{ref}")
   173	    if not remote:
   174	        problems.append(f"origin/{ref} does not resolve")
   175	    elif subprocess.run(["git", "merge-base", "--is-ancestor", commit, f"origin/{ref}"],
   176	                        cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True).returncode != 0:
   177	        problems.append(f"{commit[:8]} is not reachable from origin/{ref} — "
   178	                        f"the push did not carry it")
   179	    return problems
   180	
   181	
   182	def _check_test(claim: dict) -> list[str]:
   183	    """The status must come from the verifier invocation itself, not from a later command."""
   184	    problems = []
   185	    if "suite" not in claim:
   186	        problems.append("test claim needs the suite identity")
   187	    if "exit_status" not in claim:
   188	        problems.append("test claim needs an exit_status captured from the suite itself")
   189	    elif claim["exit_status"] != 0:
   190	        problems.append(f"{claim.get('suite')} exited {claim['exit_status']}; not a pass")
   191	    if claim.get("status_from") not in ("direct", None):
   192	        problems.append(f"exit status came from {claim['status_from']!r}, not the suite")
   193	    return problems
   194	
   195	
   196	def _check_merge(claim: dict) -> list[str]:
   197	    """No unmerged paths, no conflict markers in governed files, integrity verifying."""
   198	    problems = []
   199	    status = git("status", "--porcelain")
   200	    unmerged = [ln for ln in status.splitlines()
   201	                if ln[:2] in ("UU", "AA", "DD", "AU", "UA", "DU", "UD")]
   202	    if unmerged:
   203	        problems.append(f"{len(unmerged)} unmerged path(s) remain")
   204	    for path in GOVERNED:
   205	        f = REPO_ROOT / path
   206	        if f.is_file():
   207	            text = f.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   208	            if "\n<<<<<<< " in text or "\n>>>>>>> " in text:
   209	                problems.append(f"conflict markers in {path}")
   210	    r = subprocess.run(["python3", "tools/build_manifest.py", "corpus/raw/"],
   211	                       cwd=REPO_ROOT, capture_output=True)
   212	    if r.returncode != 0:
   213	        problems.append("manifest does not verify against corpus/raw")
   214	    return problems
   215	
   216	
   217	PROFILES = {"push": _check_push, "test": _check_test, "merge": _check_merge}
   218	
   219	
   220	def attest(action: str, claim: dict, note: str = "") -> dict:
   221	    """Verify a completion claim, LOG IT EITHER WAY, and raise if it does not hold.
   222	
   223	    Logging on failure is the point. An executive that records only its successes has an action
   224	    log that cannot be used to audit it.
   225	    """
   226	    check = PROFILES.get(action)
   227	    problems = check(claim) if check else [f"no postcondition profile for {action!r}"]
   228	    entry = log_action(action, claim, verified=not problems, problems=problems, note=note)
   229	    if problems:
   230	        raise AttestationFailed(f"{action}: " + "; ".join(problems))
   231	    return entry
   232	
   233	
   234	def log_action(action: str, claim: dict, verified: bool | None = None,
   235	               problems: list | None = None, note: str = "") -> dict:
   236	    """Append one action to the hash-chained log. Never rewrites, never deletes."""
   237	    entries = read_log()
   238	    prev = (hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(entries[-1], sort_keys=True,
   239	                                      separators=(",", ":")).encode()).hexdigest()
   240	            if entries else "0" * 64)
   241	    entry = {
   242	        "utc": utc_now(),
   243	        "action": action,
   244	        "claim": claim,
   245	        "verified": verified,
   246	        "problems": problems or [],
   247	        "note": note,
   248	        "actor": "claude-code",
   249	        "head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD")[:12],
   250	        "branch": git("rev-parse", "--abbrev-ref", "HEAD"),
   251	        #  Stamped on EVERY action, so a later reader can tell a principled stop from
   252	        #  exhaustion. Review named undisclosed quota as a hazard: it creates silent selection
   253	        #  over which duties get executed.
   254	        "quota": quota_now(),
   255	        "authority": "none — this layer adopts nothing and interprets no prohibition finally",
   256	        "prev_sha256": prev,
   257	    }
   258	    LOG_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   259	    with LOG_PATH.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
   260	        handle.write(json.dumps(entry, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   261	    return entry
   262	
   263	
   264	def read_log() -> list[dict]:
   265	    if not LOG_PATH.is_file():
   266	        return []
   267	    return [json.loads(ln) for ln in LOG_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if ln.strip()]
   268	
   269	
   270	def main() -> int:
   271	    entries = read_log()
   272	    if not entries:
   273	        print("no executive actions logged")
   274	        return 0
   275	    prev = "0" * 64
   276	    broken = 0
   277	    for e in entries:
   278	        if e["prev_sha256"] != prev:
   279	            print(f"  CHAIN BROKEN at {e['utc']} {e['action']}")
   280	            broken += 1
   281	        prev = hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(e, sort_keys=True,
   282	                                         separators=(",", ":")).encode()).hexdigest()
   283	        q = e.get("quota") or {}
   284	        fh = (q.get("five_hour") or {}).get("utilization")
   285	        mark = "ok " if e.get("verified") else ("REFUSED" if e.get("verified") is False else "—")
   286	        print(f"  {e['utc']}  {e['action']:8} {mark:8} 5h={fh}%  {e.get('note','')[:44]}")
   287	    n = len(entries)
   288	    refused = sum(1 for e in entries if e.get("verified") is False)
   289	    print(f"\n{n} action(s), {refused} refused, {broken} chain break(s)")
   290	    print(f"trial target is 10 actions — {'COMPLETE' if n >= 10 else f'{10-n} remaining'}")
   291	    return 1 if broken else 0
   292	
   293	
   294	if __name__ == "__main__":
   295	    raise SystemExit(main())
