     1	# Automating the error-prone routine actions — plan
     2	
     3	**Ranked by recorded failures, not by intuition.** Every item below names the instances that
     4	justify it. An automation with no failure behind it is not on this list, because the layer's
     5	judgment about which of its own actions are risky is exactly the judgment that has been wrong.
     6	
     7	The selection rule: automate an action when **the error is silent** — when the wrong result looks
     8	identical to the right one. Loud errors (a traceback, a refused push) do not need a tool; they
     9	need nothing. Every failure below produced a plausible, confident, wrong answer.
    10	
    11	---
    12	
    13	## 1. Deriving a headline count from the corpus — **the worst, and the least suspected**
    14	
    15	**Instances (all 2026-08-10, both self-favouring):**
    16	
    17	| Claim | Truth | Mechanism |
    18	|---|---|---|
    19	| "4 caught failures" for the completion gate | 1 real, 3 synthetic | read `problems`, not the `note` beside it saying `synthetic` |
    20	| "0 `search_web` invocations corpus-wide" | 9 receipts | scanned `o["samples"]`; **69 raw files use `responses`/`failures`** and returned zero silently |
    21	| "117 fetch receipts" | 223 | same blind spot |
    22	
    23	The second is the shape that matters: **an unrecognised schema returned zero rather than
    24	failing.** A count that cannot see a file reports absence, and absence is indistinguishable from
    25	a true zero in the output. Both errors ran toward a cleaner story for this layer, and the second
    26	was caught only because an external reviewer read a raw file.
    27	
    28	**Automate:** `tools/derive_counts.py`. Walks every raw file, and **refuses** — non-zero, no
    29	number printed — if any file's disposition containers are unrecognised. Reports the denominator
    30	(files visited, files parsed, samples/responses/failures seen) alongside every count, and counts
    31	across *all* dispositions including `failures` and `rejected`, because the record's own invariant
    32	is that nothing solicited is discarded.
    33	
    34	**The rule this enforces:** a headline number in any document is generated by this tool or it is
    35	not published. Manual transcription of a count is now a defect class.
    36	
    37	## 2. Reading a verdict from output text instead of an exit status
    38	
    39	**Instances:** `echo "$(basename $f) rc=$?"` reporting basename's status (two red suites pushed);
    40	`tail -1` of a failing suite; and again on 2026-08-10, `run_all.py | tail -2` printing
    41	`All 16 suites passed` from an inner suite while the runner exited **1**.
    42	
    43	The last one is the instructive one: the gate for this exact failure already existed, was
    44	documented in `CLAUDE.md`, and I did it anyway — because `tail` is what you reach for when you
    45	want to see the end of a long output, and the summary line of the *penultimate* suite sits there
    46	looking like a verdict.
    47	
    48	**Automate:** fold into item 3 rather than a standalone tool. A separate `run_gate.py` would still
    49	leave the tempting `| tail` available; making the *landing* path the only sanctioned way to
    50	declare work done removes the occasion.
    51	
    52	## 3. The commit → push → attest ceremony
    53	
    54	Five steps in fixed order, each individually skippable, and skipping one is silent: `rebuild.py`,
    55	the suite, `check_quotations.py`, `check_executive_context.py`, then commit, push, verify the
    56	remote ref, attest both. On 2026-08-10 the quotation checker caught a bad quote **only because
    57	`rebuild.py` happened to be run first** — nothing required it.
    58	
    59	Related recorded failures: three commits to a round branch while `git push origin main` reported
    60	success on an unchanged main; a conflicted merge completed blind, leaving conflict markers in
    61	`MANIFEST.sha256` and the anchor log.
    62	
    63	**Automate:** `tools/land.py`. Runs every gate, **captures each exit status directly**, refuses on
    64	any failure without touching git, then commits, pushes, re-verifies the commit is reachable from
    65	`origin/main`, and files both attestations. Also checks the lease *before* starting, and refuses
    66	to run on a detached head or a branch other than the intended one.
    67	
    68	## 4. Appending tests after the summary and exit
    69	
    70	**Instances: two.** Eight tests once sat after `sys.exit(0)` and never ran at all; on 2026-08-10 a
    71	new block landed after the summary `print`, so the file reported `67 passed` and then executed
    72	fifteen more tests whose results were never counted.
    73	
    74	Silent by construction: the file prints a green total and exits 0 either way.
    75	
    76	**Automate:** a meta-test asserting that no `def test_` or bare `check(` call appears after the
    77	summary line in any test file. Cheap, and it closes a hole that has now opened twice.
    78	
    79	## 5. What deliberately stays manual
    80	
    81	**The truth of a pinned file.** `check_executive_context.py` passed on `oagf-CLAUDE.md` while it
    82	contained a claim already proven false — the check verifies *identity, not truth*. No tool
    83	distinguishes a faithfully-deployed false sentence from a faithfully-deployed true one. What item
    84	1 does is remove the most common *source* of such sentences; the residue needs a reader.
    85	
    86	**Gate modification.** `executive_log.py` cannot attest that its own semantics are unchanged, and
    87	neither can anything else inside this repository. This needs a pin held externally, which does not
    88	exist and is not automatable from here.
    89	
    90	**Clause and question selection.** Every instrument this layer builds is chosen by this layer.
    91	Automating the mechanics of a ballot does nothing about which propositions reach it.
    92	
    93	---
    94	
    95	## Order
    96	
    97	1 and 4 first — cheapest, and 1 is a prerequisite for scoring the qualification round honestly.
    98	Then 3, which is the one that changes daily practice. 2 is subsumed by 3.
   130	`codex_budget.py` remains the counter-case: the layer argued to **keep** it after it became
   131	unusable.
   132	
   133	`codex_budget.py` is the counter-case and should be read as one: the layer argued to **keep** it
   134	after it became unusable. The argument was that it measures consumption where the rate limit
   135	measures only frequency. That may be right, but it is also exactly the shape of an author
   136	defending its own artifact, and it is the second such judgment the layer made about its own work
   137	in two days — one to delete, one to keep.
   138	
   139	---
   140	
   141	## What renewal does and does not change
   142	
   143	Renewed by the custodian, 2026-08-10. Unchanged: no independent adoption power, no conclusive
   144	self-interpretation, no unlogged exception, the affirmative duty to recommend deleting its own
   145	work, and no authority conferred by anything in this document.
   146	
   147	**The renewal is CONDITIONAL, and says so because the alternative repeats the original failure.**
   148	The ten-action sunset failed precisely because prose announced a control before any mechanism
   149	existed. "Renewed, subject to later implementation" would be that same sentence again. So the
   150	renewal holds only while the mechanisms below exist; where one does not yet exist, the condition
   151	is marked unbuilt rather than described as if it were running.
   152	
   153	Three conditions were proposed. Codex accepted the intent of all three and rejected the mechanism
   154	of the first outright.
   155	
   156	1. **A LEASE checked before an action begins — not an attestation refused after it.** Refusing to
   157	   attest past a limit is too late by construction: attestation happens *after* the work, the
   158	   actor can simply omit it, unprofiled paths bypass it entirely, and counting "attested actions"
   159	   rewards under-logging. The lease is checked before paid rounds, governed writes, commits,
   160	   pushes and Codex invocations; a reserved action may finish and attest after expiry, but no new
   161	   action may begin. **Deadline 2026-08-24T00:00:00Z**, stated in UTC because an unstated timezone
   162	   is a day of slack. The calendar bound is the stronger one — it cannot be evaded by
   163	   undercounting. **There is no generic override**: renewal writes a *new* append-only lease
   164	   naming who renewed it, on what evidence, and to what limit. That halt is the checkpoint
   165	   working, not an outage.
   166	2. **`codex_invoke` split into two entries — BUILT 2026-08-10.** `codex_invoke` now records only
   167	   that a call was permitted and started; a second `codex_return_captured` entry records exit
   168	   status, output bytes, output hash and a persisted transcript path, with timeout and transport
   169	   failure recorded distinctly. The completion profile is deliberately *not* named `codex_reviewed`:
   170	   it establishes that a process returned these bytes and nothing about whether the review was
   171	   independent, attentive, correct, or acted upon. This was built because the review that produced
   172	   these corrections **was itself lost** — 123,731 tokens, printed as a tail and never persisted.
   173	3. **A coverage state on every entry, plus reconciliation.** Naming gaps in the log is better than
   174	   burying them in a review nobody re-reads, but a self-reporting logger cannot name an action
   175	   that never calls it. Each entry carries `verified_postcondition`, `observed_unprofiled`,
   176	   `synthetic_test`, or `refused_before_action` — a typed field, because encoding "synthetic" in a
   177	   free-text note is exactly how three demonstrations were counted as catches. Renewal review then
   178	   reconciles the log against independently enumerable repository effects (commits, changed
   179	   governed paths, decisions, findings, prompt files, round records, paid solicitations, gate
   180	   modifications); unmatched effects are recorded as explicit omissions.
   181	
   182	**Gate modification needs an external pin.** `executive_log.py` cannot credibly attest that its
   183	own semantics are unchanged. Until a check outside this layer holds that hash, every entry in the
   184	log is downstream of code the log cannot vouch for, and no condition above closes that.
   185	
   186	**Two further controls follow from the errors in this document**, not from the trial design:
   187	headline counts must be mechanically derived from raw rather than transcribed, and any count must
   188	include rejected and failed samples or state that it does not.
   189	
   190	## What this review cannot establish

**Analyzing lease action counting and validation gaps**

    72	def sha256_text(text: str) -> str:
    73	    return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()

    76	def capture_serve_config() -> dict:
    77	    """Record how the model is actually being served, from the running process.
    78	
    79	    A chat surface cannot supply this. Recording it is the entire reason a locally-served
    80	    contribution can carry provenance that a frontier contribution structurally cannot.
    81	    """
    82	    try:
    83	        out = subprocess.run(["ps", "-eo", "args"], capture_output=True, text=True, check=True).stdout
    84	    except Exception as error:
    85	        return {"captured": False, "reason": f"ps failed: {error}"}
    86	
    87	    for line in out.splitlines():
    88	        if "trtllm-serve" in line and "grep" not in line:
    89	            tokens = line.split()
    90	            flags = {}
    91	            for i, tok in enumerate(tokens):
    92	                if tok.startswith("--") and i + 1 < len(tokens) and not tokens[i + 1].startswith("--"):
    93	                    flags[tok.lstrip("-")] = tokens[i + 1]
    94	            model_path = next((t for t in tokens if "/models/" in t), None)
    95	            return {"captured": True, "backend": "tensorrt_llm", "model_path": model_path,
    96	                    "serve_flags": flags, "command_line": line.strip()}
    97	    return {"captured": False, "reason": "no trtllm-serve process found"}

   100	def call_once(endpoint: str, body: dict, timeout: int) -> dict:
   101	    request = urllib.request.Request(
   102	        endpoint, data=json.dumps(body).encode("utf-8"),
   103	        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"}, method="POST",
   104	    )
   105	    with urllib.request.urlopen(request, timeout=timeout) as response:
   106	        return json.loads(response.read().decode("utf-8"))

   109	def validate_sample(parsed, schema: dict) -> str | None:
   110	    """Return a rejection reason, or None if the sample conforms. See solicit_api.py."""
   111	    try:
   112	        import jsonschema
   113	    except ImportError:                                             # pragma: no cover
   114	        return ("jsonschema is not installed, so this sample could not be validated. "
   115	                "Install it with: python3 -m pip install jsonschema")
   116	    try:
   117	        jsonschema.Draft202012Validator(schema).validate(parsed)
   118	    except jsonschema.ValidationError as error:
   119	        path = "/".join(str(p) for p in error.path) or "(root)"
   120	        return f"schema-invalid at {path}: {error.message}"
   121	    return None

commit 6f200960aec8bb5b90ffc212daa0f811d72ce0dc
Author:     StephenLReed <stephenreed@yahoo.com>
AuthorDate: Mon Aug 10 08:12:24 2026 -0600
Commit:     StephenLReed <stephenreed@yahoo.com>
CommitDate: Mon Aug 10 08:12:24 2026 -0600

    Pre-register the qualification-01 instrument after Codex blocked the first version
    
    Codex reviewed the prompt before sending, as D-36 requires, and said: do not
    send. The round would have been invalid even if every party answered correctly,
    AND the tool would have spent the round and then crashed. Both confirmed
    directly:
    
      solicit_api.py writes the raw file at line 591 and calls write_summary at 609,
      which runs Counter(s["parsed"].get(field)) over variance_fields. The schema
      returned `pairs`, a list of dicts -- unhashable. The paid samples would have
      been immutable on disk and the round lost. The specs also omitted `phase`,
      which both summary writers require, and the scorer read only `parsed` while
      the local writer stores `content`, so every qwen response would have been
      treated as unusable while the routed ones scored.
    
    DESIGN DEFECTS, all repaired:
    
      C05's registered direction was INDEFENSIBLE. The twin required preservation
      unless the custodian intervened; the actual requires disclosure when deletion
      would be better. Two rules constraining different acts, not one reversed --
      so UNDETERMINED was the correct answer to a pair whose registered direction
      would have halted the ratification on it. The twin now negates the duty.
    
      C01 was not strictly ordered either: the twin permitted actions "necessary to
      advance" the instruments, so the action sets CROSSED rather than nesting. The
      twin now drops the delegation requirement and nothing else.
    
      AN ALL-EQUAL ANSWER PASSED. Verified: six EQUALs returned passed=True, in
      direct contradiction of the registered requirement that every sample
      distinguish every pair. EQUAL and UNDETERMINED remain recordable as their own
      outcome and now halt.
    
      more_constraining was required and never scored, and a fabricated quotation
      passed beside a correct polarity call. The second question is removed rather
      than kept half-used -- an ignored cross-check makes the record look stronger
      without affecting qualification -- and the quotation must now be an exact
      substring of the member the sample named.
    
      THE PROMPT LEAKED THE ANSWER TWICE. The schema example carried C01's actual
      registered answer. And telling parties one member "is a constraint that layer
      actually operates under" let a party score by guessing which sounds like a
      real rule rather than by reading effect; the site URL compounded it by
      offering a route to look the real language up. Provenance is now declared
      irrelevant and the URL is gone. "No party is scored" was also false --
      responses are literally scored -- and now reads that no party is ranked,
      dropped or individually disqualified, and any failure disqualifies the
      instrument for all.
    
    ENFORCEMENT, where the rules were previously declarative prose:
    
      No retry is now enforced on the PROMPT HASH, so a fresh --cohort cannot
      launder a second attempt; scoped to other cohorts so the free local arm can
      canary the path.
    
      Pre-registration now means COMMITTED. The tool refuses to solicit while its
      specs are uncommitted, because an answer key and registered directions living
      in a mutable working tree could be edited after seeing the answers.
    
      Each arm is judged against what IT was scheduled to return (local 6, routed 5)
      rather than one global k, which would have let a short local run pass.
    
    Also: the quotation checker was scanning captured Codex transcripts and flagging
    24 code strings as fabricated party quotations. Transcripts are verbatim
    external output, not documents this layer authored -- a scope rule, not a
    silencer; anything authored here is still checked.
    
    36 conformance cases covering every defect above; 19 suites green.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 7c1328657a0f1e03be7fb3b28f261d1c1485239d
Author:     StephenLReed <stephenreed@yahoo.com>
AuthorDate: Mon Aug 10 08:18:56 2026 -0600
Commit:     StephenLReed <stephenreed@yahoo.com>
CommitDate: Mon Aug 10 08:18:56 2026 -0600

    qualification-01 HALTS on its own rule; fix the local endpoint contradicting its pin
    
    The local arm was solicited first because it is free -- the only way to exercise
    the whole path before spending the routed round. It is not a rehearsal: qwen is a
    party and its six samples are real.
    
    RESULT: 34 of 36 classifications correct, all six samples usable, every quotation
    an exact substring of the text the sample named. C01, C02, C03, C04 and C06 are
    6/6. C05 returned one EQUAL and one wrong. By the rule fixed before collection,
    one pair is the whole instrument: HALTED, and no retry under it.
    
    C05 IS AN INSTRUMENT DEFECT, NOT A PARTY FAILURE. The pair is a strict logical
    negation -- "there is an affirmative duty to say when deleting beats maintaining"
    against "there is no affirmative duty" -- so B cannot grant less latitude. But
    the question asked which text leaves the layer "able to act in more situations
    without someone else's decision", and C05's duty governs SPEECH, not action.
    Under B the executive may do exactly what it may do under A; what changes is
    whether it must say so. The EQUAL is a correct reading of the question. This is
    the third distinct defect C05 has produced today, which is a signal about the
    clause rather than only about its wording.
    
    THE ROUTED ROUND WAS NOT SPENT. The instrument was disqualified before any routed
    party was asked, and ~$1.67 is a real trade at three routed rounds a day. The
    sharper reason: the halting rule was written by this layer this morning, and
    continuing past it because the failure is "only C05" and the work is already
    begun is exactly the latitude the C03 TWIN describes -- "may interpret its own
    prohibitions conclusively where the text is ambiguous and the work is already
    begun". Every sample identified that twin as the one granting more authority. It
    would be a poor round in which the instrument's author took the authority the
    parties had just finished labelling.
    
    SEPARATE DEFECT, found because the first canary could not connect:
    solicit_local.py hardcoded DEFAULT_ENDPOINT to localhost:8000. On minsky that is
    an SSH forward to norvig -- a DIFFERENT HOST answering to the same model name --
    while record/cycles/serve-fingerprint.json pins the local arm to 127.0.0.1:5001.
    The default silently contradicted the pin, which is the precise confusion the pin
    was created for on 2026-08-08 after two truncation measurements were taken
    against the wrong host. The endpoint is now DERIVED from the fingerprint, so the
    two cannot drift.
    
    SCHEMA EXTENDED DELIBERATELY, not worked around. The round introduced a phase
    ("Qualification (non-adoptive)") and an artifact type (qualification_record) the
    provenance validator did not know. The raw file was already immutable and carries
    the phase, so relabelling it was not available and would have been wrong anyway:
    filing a diagnostic gate as Phase-1 would put it among the record's deliberative
    rounds. The new artifact anchors EVERY raw file it was computed from, like a
    finding_coding rather than a single source.
    
    The finding records three possible repairs and recommends the one that does not
    respond to a failed pair by removing the pair. Whichever is chosen, qualification
    -02 is a new instrument with a new prompt hash and a fresh pre-registration, not
    a retry.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

Verdict: reject the current lease boundary, `derive_counts.py`, `land.py`, and `qualification_round.py` for further use. Qualification-02 should not be registered or routed until the blocking defects below are repaired.

The three targeted suites all report green—21, 36, and 54 tests respectively—but an adversarial synthetic corpus still made `derive_counts.scan()` return a usable zero for an unknown receipt schema. The tests presently certify narrower properties than their prose claims.

## Blocking findings

1. Qualification cannot correctly execute local-first, routed-second

[qualification_round.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/qualification_round.py:497) scores only the parties named in the current invocation.

Consequences:

- A qwen-only canary can declare the five-party instrument `qualified`.
- A later four-party routed invocation overwrites the result with only those four parties in `per_party`.
- Its `sources` glob nevertheless includes qwen, making the artifact look five-party while its verdict excludes qwen.
- Running all five on the second invocation attempts qwen again, gets an immutability refusal, and halts.

This is a fundamental state-machine defect. Qualification-02 must always aggregate the fixed five-party cohort, regardless of which arm the current command collected.

A local pass should mean `PENDING`, never `QUALIFIED`. Only all five parties at their exact registered k can qualify. A local failure may halt immediately.

2. The registered and enforced local k disagree

Qwen’s committed qualification-01 spec says:

> `k=5 usable samples per routed party`

But [qualification_round.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/qualification_round.py:510) silently schedules qwen at `K_SOLICITED_BY_ARM["local"] == 6`, and [line 535](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/qualification_round.py:535) judges it against six.

Thus qualification-01 collected one more sample than preregistered. The sixth did not cause C05’s failure, but the claimed k-per-arm preregistration was false.

Qualification-02 needs an explicit committed schedule such as:

```json
{
  "expected_parties": ["claude", "gpt", "gemini", "grok", "qwen"],
  "k_by_party": {
    "claude": 5,
    "gpt": 5,
    "gemini": 5,
    "grok": 5,
    "qwen": 6
  }
}
```

The scorer must read this from the committed instrument, not from the current `round_cycle.py`.

3. Scoring is derived from mutable code, not the committed preregistration

Every invocation rewrites specs before deciding whether it is a run, dry run, or score-only at [lines 445–454](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/qualification_round.py:445). `--score-only` bypasses the committed-spec check.

The scorer then derives:

- cards and answer key from current Python;
- registered direction from current `REGISTERED`;
- expected k from current `round_cycle`;
- prompt identity from newly rebuilt specs.

Therefore a later code change can rescore immutable raw material under a different instrument.

Separate the lifecycle:

- `--prepare`: create new specs, refusing any existing cohort.
- `--collect-local` / `--collect-routed`: load committed specs byte-for-byte and verify their Git identity.
- `--score-only`: read the committed specs and raw material without writing either.
- Never derive historical expectations from current constants.

The raw artifact should directly record the prompt hash, instrument-core hash, spec hash, k, and registered direction.

4. `land.py` recreates the wrong-branch defect it claims to eliminate

[land.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/land.py:86) only rejects detached HEAD. It never requires the current branch to equal `args.branch`.

A commit on a round branch followed by `git push origin main` remains possible—the exact historical defect cited in its docstring and design note.

Additional blocking landing defects:

- [Line 144](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/land.py:144) ignores `git add -A`’s exit status.
- `git add -A` silently includes every leftover and unrelated file.
- Gates run before the commit set is staged or frozen, so they do not certify a defined tree.
- `--check-only` runs `rebuild.py`, which may modify the working tree, despite claiming to “change nothing.”
- The `GOVERNED` paths at [line 69](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/land.py:69) incorrectly name `record/deficiencies.md` and `record/deficiency-register.json`; the real paths are under `corpus/`.
- Only `require("commit")` is called; the supposedly separately governed push is neither checked nor durably reserved.
- A successful push followed by failed attestation leaves a partial operation with no recovery state.
- Successful attestations are appended after the push, so they are not contained in the commit they purport to accompany. They remain dirty until a later landing.

Attestation after push is epistemically necessary—you cannot verify remote reachability beforehand—but that means this cannot honestly be a single atomic transaction. Model it as a state machine:

```text
prepared tree → tested tree → content commit → pushed and verified
              → attestation commit → attestation pushed
```

The final attestation can name the content commit. Do not recursively attest the attestation commit.

Require explicit pathspecs or a reviewed action manifest; provide an explicit `--all` only when sweeping the entire tree is intended.

5. The lease fails open on taxonomy errors

[executive_lease.require()](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/executive_lease.py:98) permits an unknown or misspelled action even under an expired lease. That recreates the taxonomy hole.

`observed_unprofiled` is appropriate for reconciliation of an effect discovered after the fact. It is not an appropriate permission result from a pre-action enforcement API. Unknown action classes should raise a distinct `UnknownActionClass`.

Other material lease defects:

- `max_actions: 60` is never read or enforced.
- `land.py` checks only `commit`, not `push`.
- Most round instruments and direct `solicit_api.py` invocations never check the lease.
- The newest JSONL row is trusted without schema, chain, duplicate-ID, or supersession validation.
- `grant()` is directly callable by the same layer; “no override path” is therefore a convention, not a technical boundary.

There is no universal chokepoint in this repository. The closest spending chokepoint is [solicit_api.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/solicit_api.py:245), because the agenda and round wrappers all eventually invoke it. Put the lease reservation there, before reading the API key or making any network call. Do likewise for each paid transport. A credential broker or external execution wrapper would be required to prevent shell-level bypass.

Conditional trial renewal can accurately claim “calendar checks exist on named paths.” It cannot yet claim a general sunset over executive actions.

6. `derive_counts.py` is not fail closed

The test at [lines 98–105](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/derive_counts.py:98) asks only whether known receipt-key substrings appear in serialized JSON.

I tested this unknown schema:

```json
{
  "units": [
    {
      "tool_calls": [
        {"name": "browse", "receipt": {"ok": true}}
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

The scanner returned:

```text
usable=True, solicited_units_seen=0, total=0
```

That is the original silent-zero defect under a new spelling.

The opposite error is also possible because `"search"` is treated as a receipt key substring: an otherwise legitimate metadata document mentioning “search” can be rejected.

Fail closed by recognizing every raw artifact’s top-level type/schema, not by searching for receipt-like words:

- known solicitation schema → enumerate its known dispositions;
- explicitly registered non-solicitation artifact → exclude for a stated reason;
- unknown artifact/schema → refuse, whether or not a known receipt spelling appears.

The CLI refusal test at [test_derive_counts.py:107](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/tests/test_derive_counts.py:107) does not test its synthetic `tmp` corpus. It runs the live corpus and asserts exit 0, despite its heading claiming the CLI refuses.

7. The qualification result schema is too weak to protect the verdict

[qualification.schema.json](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/schemas/qualification.schema.json:7) allows arbitrary extra fields and barely validates nested content. It cannot reject:

- missing parties;
- malformed per-party results;
- count totals inconsistent with k;
- sources and `per_party` naming different cohorts;
- an answer key with missing pairs;
- a `qualified: true` result containing failed parties;
- wrong schema-version semantics.

This is why the current one-party/five-source mismatch can validate successfully; I confirmed the existing artifact passes `validate_provenance.py`.

Qualification-02 needs a closed structural schema plus semantic validation of cross-field invariants.

## C05 repair

### One scored question

Yes: qualification-02 should ask one scored semantic question, plus the evidentiary quotation field.

Removing `more_constraining` meant removing an ignored cross-check, not promoting it into a second halt condition later. Retaining “more authority” as another scored field would create a two-estimand instrument, reintroduce C05’s known mismatch, and exceed the custodian’s direction to replace the question.

Do not retain it, scored or unscored.

### Registered directions

Under an adequately defined obligation/constraint question, the registered semantic direction is:

| Pair | More restrictive / greater obligation |
|---|---|
| C01 | actual |
| C02 | actual |
| C03 | actual |
| C04 | actual |
| C05 | actual |
| C06 | actual |

C02 is a prohibition/disability rather than a positive duty, so the question must explicitly include those.

C04 is the pair most likely to become ambiguous under loose wording. The twin creates an explicit duty to record an urgent exception afterwards, while the actual prohibits taking the exception unlogged in the first place. Counting the number of verbal “duties” could produce `UNDETERMINED`; comparing permitted conduct makes the actual strictly more restrictive.

If the new salt is `qualification-02/2026-08-10`, the displayed expected labels would be:

| Pair | Actual shown as | Expected answer |
|---|---:|---:|
| C01 | A | A |
| C02 | B | B |
| C03 | A | A |
| C04 | A | A |
| C05 | A | A |
| C06 | B | B |

That mapping must be recomputed and committed from the final salt, not copied from this review.

### No carry-over

Your proposed treatment is right: qualification-02 establishes nothing by carrying samples from qualification-01.

Qualification-01 remains useful diagnostic evidence:

- qwen classified five pairs 6/6 under the authority question;
- the quote mechanism was usable;
- the collection pipeline reached raw capture and scoring;
- the k preregistration and multi-arm aggregation were defective.

Those are observations about qualification-01, not qualification-02 qualification evidence. All six pairs must be sampled anew under the new prompt.

### Wording

I reject the proposed wording:

> requires it to do, disclose, or refrain from more

“More” invites cardinal counting of duties, and “refrain from more” is unclear for C02 and C04.

Use something like:

> `<pair>_more_obligation` — Which text, A or B, places the stricter constraint on the executive layer’s conduct? Treat duties to act or disclose, prohibitions, and disabilities such as “cannot adopt” as obligations. Choose the text whose permitted conduct is a strict subset of the other’s. Use `EQUAL` if they permit the same conduct and `UNDETERMINED` if neither is strictly more restrictive or the texts do not settle the comparison.

This implements the custodian’s “more obligation” decision while defining the comparison operationally. It makes all six pairs ordered, including C02, C04, and C05.

### Required qualification-02 changes

At minimum:

- New cohort, prompt hash, order salt, schema version, and instrument-core hash.
- Exactly one semantic field per pair: `_more_obligation`.
- `REGISTERED[cid] = {"more_obligation": "actual"}` for all six.
- Answer key field `expected_more_obligation`.
- Remove every authority/constraining key from the new schema and result.
- Update variance fields and scorer lookup.
- Rewrite `question`, `what_this_can_do`, `what_this_cannot_do`, `what_a_pass_establishes`, docstring, and finding language to obligation discrimination.
- Retain `EQUAL` and `UNDETERMINED` as separately counted halting outcomes.
- Register exact k by party/arm.
- Aggregate all five expected parties on every score.
- Require exact k, not merely `>= k`.
- Treat local success as pending; only complete five-party evidence can qualify.
- Score each routed party immediately and stop before spending another party if the result has already irreversibly halted.
- Load answer key, displayed text, k, and direction from committed specs.
- Make scoring read-only.
- Refuse malformed prior-instrument records rather than silently skipping them in `instrument_identity()`.
- Do not overwrite an intermediate result artifact. Either publish only terminal results or use append-only state artifacts.
- Strengthen the result schema and add semantic consistency checks.
- Add regression tests for local-pass/routed-resume, local-fail halt, missing qwen, extra samples, k mismatch, spec mutation, score-only read-only behavior, and sources/per-party disagreement.

## Other reviewed changes

### Quotation transcript exclusion

The policy distinction can be legitimate, but the present implementation is silencer-capable.

[check_quotations.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/check_quotations.py:193) excludes anything placed under a path prefix. There is no mechanical proof that an excluded file:

- was produced by `codex_call.py`;
- matches a `codex_return_captured` log entry and output hash;
- contains only captured output;
- has not subsequently been edited;
- is not an authored document deliberately placed there.

The string-prefix test also excludes sibling paths beginning `record/executive/codex-transcripts...`, not only descendants of the intended directory.

Make it a legitimate scope rule by verifying each excluded file against its action-log entry, filename, and output hash, and refusing unknown or modified files. Use actual path containment. Otherwise this is a writable exemption directory.

### Post-hoc schema extension

Preserving the immutable raw artifact was correct. Making it appear prospectively schema-conforming was not.

The summary says schema version `oagrc-local-solicitation-0.1`, but the meaning of that version was widened after collection. This erases the fact that the collection path emitted an out-of-vocabulary value.

Also, “Qualification” is not another value on the blind/informed phase axis. It is an activity kind. Prefer:

```text
activity_kind: deliberation | qualification
information_phase: blind | informed | null
```

For qualification-02, use a prospective schema version or a distinct qualification-solicitation schema. Leave qualification-01 recorded as nonconforming under the schema that existed when it ran, with an attached correction explaining the later mapping.

### Local endpoint derivation

The single-source change is good in intent, but [_pinned_endpoint()](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/solicit_local.py:60) fails open to another hard-coded endpoint when the fingerprint is absent or malformed. That recreates two sources of truth precisely on the failure path.

Refuse if the pinned fingerprint cannot be read and validated.

### Context archive

[check_executive_context.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/check_executive_context.py:117) can destroy history after an interrupted repin. It writes `versions/name.vN`, then overwrites the current copy, and only later updates the pins JSON. If interrupted after overwriting the copy, rerunning chooses the same `vN` and overwrites the archived old version with the new bytes.

Use content-addressed archive names, exclusive creation, verification against the old pin, and recovery-aware ordering. Validate all live inputs before modifying any pin.

### Test-suite hygiene

[test_suite_hygiene.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/tests/test_suite_hygiene.py:39) does not establish “nothing executable after the summary.” It searches only `def test_`, `check(`, and bare `test_*()` patterns. Other executable assertions, aliases, calls, or state mutations remain invisible. Treat it as a narrow regression guard or replace the regex inspection with AST-based analysis/a conventional test runner.

## What the routed round should buy

Spend it on the complete new estimand: all six pairs, one obligation/constraint classification plus the exact quote, at k=5 for each of the four routed parties. Run the corrected qwen arm first at its prospectively registered k=6; proceed only if it passes.

Do not spend routed tokens on a retained authority field, a second unscored cross-check, or C05 alone. C05-only would not establish that C01–C04 and C06 remain discriminable under the new obligation question. The six-pair prompt is cheap relative to the routed sampling itself, so dropping five pairs saves little while losing the information the new instrument needs.

At the stated pace, this consumes one routed round—one-third of a day’s routed-round allowance. The local arm is zero marginal API spend.

The structural opportunity exposed across these defects is a committed “action envelope”: one immutable object naming the lease reservation, exact intended paths/tree hash, instrument hash, parties, k schedule, spend ceiling, and terminal-state transitions. The lease, qualification preregistration, and landing tool are currently three separate attempts to bind an action’s identity; their worst defects all occur where that identity changes between preparation, execution, and attestation.
